### CLM-C08-01: Every shifted Knuth product a o_t b = sum F(k+l+t) over Zeckendorf indices is associative (t >= 0)

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib (Infinity.C08.circ_assoc, circ_comm, key, window; standard axioms only) for all t >= 0; counterexamples for s = 0, 1 by computation; no failure in 20,000 random triples up to 1e9 (t = 0)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For every t >= 0 the operation a o_t b = sum_{k in Z(a)} sum_{l in Z(b)} F_(k+l+t) on the natural numbers
  (Z(n) the Zeckendorf index set, indices >= 2) is associative and commutative; in the lab's G-indexing these
  are the shifts s = t + 2 >= 2. For s = 0 and s = 1 associativity fails ((2 o 4) o 4 != 2 o (4 o 4) for s = 0,
  (1 o 4) o 4 != 1 o (4 o 4) for s = 1).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C08.md#theorem-1-clm-c08-01--every-shifted-knuth-product-is-associative
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C08_Knuth.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C08-01; EXP-C08-03; EXP-C08-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  Knuth 1988; Arnoux 1989; all shifts s >= 2 and the failures at s = 0, 1 are in A. Messaoudi, Math. Slovaca 50 (2000), Thm 6 and Prop 1 (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md). The Lean proof is a verification asset.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C08-02: Circle products over non-Pisot base sequences are never associative

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  If the minimal linear recurrence of an increasing base sequence G (G_0 = 1) has a dominant root alpha > 1 and
  another characteristic root beta with |beta| >= 1 (counted with multiplicity: a repeated dominant root counts as
  such a beta), then the circle product o_s is non-associative for every s >= 0.

- **evidence:** EXP-C08-02; EXP-C08-07
- **novelty_basis:**

  As a conjecture, low confidence - not found; natural attack via Messaoudi's Cor 1 (window stability); related: property (F) implies Pisot (Frougny-Solomyak 1992) (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **limitations:**

  10 non-Pisot sequences, shifts 0..8 (EXP-C08-02); the 10 again with certified failures at every s <= 12 (EXP-C08-07); the Skeptic adds 8 more non-Pisot sequences and the double root (x - 2)^2, all failing at every tested shift (s <= 25, resp. 12). The condition alpha > 1 is needed - G_k = k + 1 (minimal polynomial (x - 1)^2) gives a o_s b = a + b + s - 1, associative at every shift; the first wording, without it, was false (Skeptic review 2026-10-18).

- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → POTENTIALLY NOVEL (sync:C08; Historian audit 2026-10-18 (reviews/historian_C03_C10.md))

### CLM-C08-03: Recurrences with non-increasing coefficients and canonical initial values give associative circle products for large shifts

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For base sequences G_k = a_1 G_(k-1) + ... + a_d G_(k-d) with a_1 >= a_2 >= ... >= a_d >= 1 and canonical
  initial values (the conditions of Grabner-Petho-Tichy-Woeginger 1994), the circle product o_s is
  associative once the shift s is large enough. (The earlier title, "Pisot base sequences ...", overstated
  the published theorem.)

- **evidence:** EXP-C08-02; EXP-C08-03; EXP-C08-06; EXP-C08-07
- **novelty_basis:**

  Published theorem (P. J. Grabner, A. Petho, R. F. Tichy, G. J. Woeginger, Appl. Math. Lett. 7 (1994) 85-90); see also A. Messaoudi, Math. Slovaca 50 (2000) 135-148 (window criterion, Thm 2-3). Not re-proved by the lab; our data agree for the sequences tested. The sequence G(k+1) = G(k) + G(k-3) (no associative shift <= 20) may be outside the theorem's hypotheses (Historian audit, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **limitations:**

  The lab's supporting thresholds were twice too low (CLM-C08-05, CLM-C08-07); the targeted search EXP-C08-07 still finds a first associative shift for every Pisot sequence of the atlas except G(k+1) = G(k) + G(k-3), which fails at every s <= 16 (the Skeptic - s <= 24) and seems to lie outside the theorem's hypotheses (its coefficients 1, 0, 0, 1 are not non-increasing).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C08-04: Non-monotone associative-shift sets require a negative conjugate root

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For quadratic Pisot base sequences G(k+1) = pG(k) + qG(k-1) with non-canonical initial values (for canonical
  ones the associative shifts are upward closed whatever the sign of beta, by Messaoudi's Thm 2 as derived by
  the Historian): if the conjugate root beta is positive, the set of shifts s for which o_s is associative is
  upward-closed; sets that are not upward-closed (e.g. {2} and s >= 4 for G = 1,3,4,7,11,...) occur only when
  beta < 0.

- **evidence:** EXP-C08-07; EXP-C08-06; EXP-C08-04; EXP-C08-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence. Not found in Knuth 1988, Arnoux 1989, Grabner et al. 1994, Messaoudi 2000 (read in full) or Ambroz-Frougny-Masakova-Pelantova 2003. The Historian derives from Messaoudi's Thm 2 that for canonical (Parry) sequences the associative shifts are upward-closed whatever the sign of beta; so the phenomenon concerns non-canonical initial values only (reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **limitations:**

  Re-tested with certificates (EXP-C08-06, the same 90 quadratic sequences, shifts 0..10) - 6 rows of EXP-C08-04 listed shifts that are certified non-associative, including two of the original non-monotone examples ((4,3;1,5) at s = 4, (3,3;1,2) at s = 7). After the re-test 10 rows are still non-monotone, all with non-canonical initial values and beta < 0; none of the 30 rows with beta > 0. Isolated 'associative' shifts are supported by search only (no proof). Note that for G(k+1) = pG(k) + qG(k-1) with q > 0 the conjugate root is automatically negative. The Skeptic's review found the evidence of EXP-C08-04 contaminated - 31 of its 90 rows (8 with beta > 0) list certified-failing shifts as associative, and 4 of its 10 non-monotone rows are artefacts. After the targeted search EXP-C08-07 (shifts 0..12) 8 rows are non-monotone, all with beta < 0 and non-canonical initial values, and none of the 30 beta > 0 rows; the Skeptic's stronger search finds 0 of 231 beta > 0 sequences non-monotone (s <= 16) and 60 of 396 beta < 0 ones, none canonical.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C08-05: Measured associativity thresholds for 46 base sequences (refuted - five of the eight quoted thresholds were too small)

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  Smallest shift s0 with o_s associative for all tested s0 <= s <= max tested: Fibonacci 2, Pell (1,2,5) 1,
  G(k+1)=2G(k)+2G(k-1) (1,2) 5, tribonacci 3, 4-bonacci 7, G(k+1)=G(k)+G(k-2) 10, G(k+1)=3G(k)+3G(k-1) (1,5) 10;
  G(k+1)=G(k)+G(k-3) none up to 20 (full table in results/EXP-C08-02.json and EXP-C08-03.json).

- **evidence:** EXP-C08-02; EXP-C08-03; EXP-C08-06; EXP-C08-07
- **counterexample:**

  Certified violations (EXP-C08-06) - tribonacci s = 3: (10016 o 10016) o 1 = 3414681926 != 3414681924 = 10016 o (10016 o 1) (the example of Ambroz-Frougny-Masakova-Pelantova 2003); 4-bonacci s = 7: triple (623729, 581581, 1); G(k+1) = 2G(k) + 2G(k-1) (1,2) s = 5: triple (29957, 280628, 1); G(k+1) = G(k) + G(k-2) s = 10: triple (78, 248, 1) (found by the Historian). The random tests behind the measurement (triples up to 10^6) missed these rare failures. The Skeptic's review adds G(k+1) = 3G(k) + 3G(k-1) (1,5) at s = 10: (173938 o 173938) o 1 != 173938 o (173938 o 1) - the fifth wrong value. Thresholds after the targeted search in CLM-C08-08.

- **novelty_basis:** Partial rediscovery - thresholds known in the literature (Messaoudi 2000, 2002; Ambroz-Frougny-Masakova-Pelantova 2003 refute Arnoux's conjecture that tribonacci s = 3 works).
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z verification: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED → REFUTED (sync:C08; Historian audit and certified re-test 2026-10-18 - four listed thresholds are wrong)
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C08; Historian audit and certified re-test 2026-10-18 - four listed thresholds are wrong)

### CLM-C08-07: Corrected associativity thresholds - certified lower bounds and searched ranges

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **proof_check:**

  lower bounds certified by explicit triples (results/EXP-C08-06.json); upper bounds proved only where the literature proves them (Fibonacci s >= 2 Lean; Pell s >= 1 Messaoudi 2000 Thm 1; tribonacci s >= 6 per Messaoudi 2002 as reported by Ambroz-Masakova-Pelantova 2007, s >= 9 GPTW; 4-bonacci s >= 10 Messaoudi 2000 Remark 2)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For the circle product o_s: Fibonacci (1,2) fails for s <= 1 and is associative for s >= 2 (Lean, CLM-C08-01);
  Pell (1,2,5) fails at s = 0, no failure found for 1 <= s <= 5 (associative for s >= 1 by Messaoudi 2000);
  G(k+1) = 2G(k) + 2G(k-1) (1,2) fails for s <= 5, no failure found for 6 <= s <= 8; tribonacci (1,2,4) fails
  for s <= 3, no failure found for 4 <= s <= 9; 4-bonacci (1,2,4,8) fails for s <= 7, no failure found for
  8 <= s <= 10; G(k+1) = G(k) + G(k-2) (1,2,3) fails for s <= 10, no failure found for s = 11, 12;
  G(k+1) = 3G(k) + 3G(k-1) (1,5) fails for s <= 9, no failure found for 10 <= s <= 12. Every failure is
  certified by an explicit triple; every "no failure" is computational support only.

- **evidence:** EXP-C08-06; EXP-C08-07
- **counterexample:**

  Two of its 'no failure found' ranges were wrong (Skeptic review 2026-10-18, reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md, P2) - G(k+1) = 2G(k) + 2G(k-1) (1,2) fails at s = 6: (314965 o 314965) o 1 != 314965 o (314965 o 1); G(k+1) = 3G(k) + 3G(k-1) (1,5) fails at s = 10 and s = 11: (173938 o 173938) o 1 != 173938 o (173938 o 1). Both re-verified with the lab's code and re-found by EXP-C08-07 (e.g. (5653, 42197, 1) at s = 6; (3192, 173938, 1) at s = 10; (12100, 12100, 1) at s = 11). The certified lower bounds of this claim stand; the thresholds it implied for these two sequences do not. Superseded by CLM-C08-08.

- **novelty_basis:** Partial - the tribonacci and 4-bonacci bounds are in the literature (Messaoudi 2000, 2002; AFMP 2003); the other values are the lab's measurements.
- **limitations:**

  Search - all pairs a <= b < 300, 4000 random pairs below 10^6, literature and Historian examples, c < 300 for an explicit triple; 'no failure found' does not prove associativity. Tribonacci s = 4 and s = 5 are open in the sources read (the Historian derives s >= 5 from AFMP's bound on fractional digits and Messaoudi's Thm 2, unchecked). The lab's second attempt at these thresholds repeated the first one's mistake - searching where failures are rare.

- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T18:17:35Z verification: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED → REFUTED (sync:C08; Skeptic review 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md) - certified failures inside two of the 'no failure found' ranges)

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For the circle product o_s: Fibonacci (1,2) fails for s <= 1, associative for s >= 2 (Lean, CLM-C08-01); Pell
  (1,2,5) fails at s = 0 (associative for s >= 1, Messaoudi 2000); G(k+1) = 2G(k) + 2G(k-1) (1,2) fails for
  s <= 6; G(k+1) = 3G(k) + 3G(k-1) (1,5) fails for s <= 11; tribonacci (1,2,4) fails for s <= 3; 4-bonacci
  (1,2,4,8) fails for s <= 7; G(k+1) = G(k) + G(k-2) (1,2,3) fails for s <= 10; G(k+1) = G(k) + G(k-3) (1,2,3,4)
  fails for every s <= 16 (the Skeptic - every s <= 24). Every failure is certified by an explicit triple. Above

- **limitations:**

  Two earlier versions of these thresholds (CLM-C08-05, CLM-C08-07) were refuted because failures are rare among random or small inputs. The targeted search is heuristic - its grid cannot see failures within one cell of the fractal window boundary (the tribonacci failure at s = 3 is found only through the literature seed), so the upper values may still be too low.


### CLM-C09-01: Knuth's circle semigroup embeds in Z[phi]; Fibonacci numbers are its units-images

- **cycle:** C09
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C09.md Theorem 1) relying on CLM-C08-01, skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); formulas checked on 89,401 pairs; the Skeptic checked the window and n* = floor((n+1)/phi) exactly for all n <= 1e8

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  (N>=1, o) with Knuth's product is a commutative cancellative semigroup without identity. The map
  nu(n) = n phi + n*, n* = floor((n+1)/phi), is an injective homomorphism into (Z[phi], x); a o b = ab + ab* + a*b.
  nu(n) is a unit iff n is a Fibonacci number F_k (k >= 2), and F_j o F_k = F_(j+k).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C09.md#theorem-1-clm-c09-01--structure
- **evidence:** EXP-C09-03
- **novelty_basis:** Arnoux 1989; the product formula is Lunnon's formula in OEIS A101330 (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).
- **status history:**

### CLM-C09-03: Window obstruction: at least a proportion 1/phi^2 of all integers are circle-irreducible

- **cycle:** C09
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C09.md Theorem 3; Weyl equidistribution), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); 0 counterexamples up to 1e6 (the Skeptic - up to 1e8), forced density 0.381967 at 1e6

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  If n is a circle product then nu'(n) = n psi + n* lies in (psi^3, psi^2), i.e. frac((n+1)/phi) lies in
  (1/phi - psi^2, 1/phi - psi^3) = (0.2360..., 0.8541...). Hence every n with frac((n+1)/phi) outside this
  interval is circle-irreducible, and these n have natural density exactly 1/phi^2 = 0.381966...

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C09.md#theorem-3-clm-c09-03--the-window-obstruction-and-density-1φ
- **evidence:** EXP-C09-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  Irreducibles/reducibles of Knuth's product are not in the OEIS (2026-10-18); no study of circle-product factorisation found in the C08 literature search. Elementary consequence of Arnoux's embedding; may exist in the literature on factorisation in submonoids of rings of integers.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C09-07: The norm N(n) = n*^2 + n n* - n^2 is multiplicative for the circle product

- **cycle:** C09
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

### CLM-C10-01: Average cost of counting in classical representations (b/(b-1), e-1, 1)

- **cycle:** C10
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C10.md Theorem 1); confirmed to 6 digits for n < 1e6
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The average number of digit changes per increment is b/(b-1) in base b, 2 in negabinary, 3/2 in balanced
  ternary, e - 1 in the factorial base and exactly 1 in the binary-reflected Gray code.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C10.md#theorem-1-clm-c10-01--classical-constants
- **evidence:** EXP-C10-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Classical (Cormen et al., ch. 17); see V. Berthe, C. Frougny, M. Rigo, J. Sakarovitch, On the cost and complexity of the successor function, Adv. Appl. Math. 2020 (BFRS) (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **notes:**

  Cost measure here: the number of digit positions that change between the representations of n and n+1 (Hamming distance). BFRS use carry propagation, under which the Zeckendorf value is phi^2 instead of 2.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C10-02: Zeckendorf counting: T_m = 2 G_m - ceil(m/2) - 2, average cost 2

- **cycle:** C10
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C10.md Theorem 2); recurrence equals brute force for m <= 24
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The total number of digit changes when counting from 0 to G_m - 1 in the Zeckendorf system (G = 1,2,3,5,...)
  is T_m = 2 G_m - ceil(m/2) - 2, satisfying T_(m+1) = T_m + T_(m-1) + ceil(m/2) + 1; the average cost of
  Zeckendorf counting tends to 2.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C10.md#theorem-2-clm-c10-02--zeckendorf-counting
- **evidence:** EXP-C10-02; EXP-C10-04
- **novelty_basis:** In substance - the closed form of T_m is OEIS A215005 (Kotesovec); potential method; see BFRS 2020 for the carry-propagation measure (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).
- **notes:** Cost measure: Hamming distance between consecutive representations (not BFRS's carry propagation).
- **status history:**

### CLM-C11-01: Pattern-terminated codes T_p are complete prefix codes with counts x^k / (x^k + (1-2x) c_p(x)); T_11 is the Fibonacci code

- **cycle:** C11
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Prefix property and unique parsing machine-checked in Lean (Infinity.C11.prefix_free, unique_parse_head; #print axioms: none); generating function and completeness by the written proof, skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); implementations checked in EXP-C11-01 (Kraft partial sums >= 0.999999999993, C1 == T_11 for n <= 2e5)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For a binary pattern p of length k, the set T_p of binary words in which p occurs for the first time at the end
  is a prefix code with Kraft sum 1, the number of its codewords of each length has generating function
  x^k / (x^k + (1 - 2x) c_p(x)) with c_p the autocorrelation polynomial of p, and T_11 (T_(1^k)) is exactly the
  codeword set of the Fibonacci code C1 (of its order-k generalisation).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C11.md#theorem-1-clm-c11-01--t_p-is-a-complete-prefix-code
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C11_Codes.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C11-01; EXP-C11-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Generating function: Guibas and Odlyzko (1981), string overlaps. Fibonacci code and its order-k generalisation: Apostolico and Fraenkel (1987), Fraenkel and Klein (1996); the 'N consecutive ones' generalisation is standard. Also: Lakshmanan, IEEE Trans. IT 27 (1981) (the pattern codes L(p) and recurrences for their codeword counts); Fraenkel-Klein, DAM 64 (1996) section 3, Thm 1 (L(p) is complete for every p) and Thm 2 (for unbordered p the comma-free and prefix versions coincide); Berstel-Perrin's semaphore codes (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C11-02: The all-ones terminator 1^k (and 0^k) uniquely maximises the growth rate among length-k patterns

- **cycle:** C11
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C11.md Theorem 2, from D_(1^k) - D_p = (1-2x)(c_(1^k) - c_p)), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); 0 violations among all 2044 patterns of length 2..10 (EXP-C11-02)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  Let lambda_p = 1/rho_p, with rho_p the smallest positive root of x^k + (1 - 2x) c_p(x). Among binary patterns of
  length k, 1^k and 0^k have the largest lambda_p and every other pattern has a strictly smaller one; hence
  T_(1^k) has the smallest asymptotic codeword length log2(n)/log2(lambda) among length-k terminators.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C11.md#theorem-2-clm-c11-02--the-all-ones-terminator-has-the-largest-growth-rate
- **evidence:** EXP-C11-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  In substance the Guibas-Odlyzko comparison of patterns through their correlation polynomials (words avoiding a more self-overlapping pattern are more numerous); the codes share the avoidance denominator. Also: Lakshmanan, IEEE Trans. IT 27 (1981) (the pattern codes L(p) and recurrences for their codeword counts); Fraenkel-Klein, DAM 64 (1996) section 3, Thm 1 (L(p) is complete for every p) and Thm 2 (for unbordered p the comma-free and prefix versions coincide); Berstel-Perrin's semaphore codes (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C11-03: Unbordered terminators: F_u = x/(1-x) F_(1^k), so codeword counts are partial sums of the all-ones counts

- **cycle:** C11
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C11.md Theorem 3), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); 0 mismatches with exact integers for all unbordered patterns of length 3..9 up to length 200 (EXP-C11-06); ratios 1.618033989, 1.191487884, 1.078095078 for k = 2, 3, 4 (EXP-C11-02)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If u is unbordered and |u| = k + 1, the codeword counts of T_u are the partial sums of those of T_(1^k):
  f_u(L) = cum_(1^k)(L - 1). Hence lambda_u is the k-bonacci constant lambda and cum_u(L)/cum_(1^k)(L) -> 1/(lambda - 1).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C11.md#theorem-3-clm-c11-03--unbordered-terminators-the-x1--x-identity
- **evidence:** EXP-C11-06; EXP-C11-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  An elementary consequence of the Guibas-Odlyzko generating function; the resulting count sequences are catalogued as partial sums (OEIS A000071 = Fibonacci(n) - 1, A008937 = partial sums of tribonacci numbers). Also: Lakshmanan, IEEE Trans. IT 27 (1981) (the pattern codes L(p) and recurrences for their codeword counts); Fraenkel-Klein, DAM 64 (1996) section 3, Thm 1 (L(p) is complete for every p) and Thm 2 (for unbordered p the comma-free and prefix versions coincide); Berstel-Perrin's semaphore codes (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C11-04: T_011 is never longer than the Fibonacci code for n >= 5 and one bit shorter on all but L values per level

- **cycle:** C11
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C11.md Corollary 4), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); exhaustive check for n <= 2e6 (difference -1 at 3 values, 0 at 429, +1 at 1999568) and block-wise check up to L = 90 (EXP-C11-06)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  cum_11(L) = F_(L+1) - 1 and cum_011(L) = F_(L+2) - L - 1. If the Fibonacci (C1) codeword of n has length L + 1,
  the T_011 codeword is one bit shorter when n <= F_(L+2) - L - 1 and of equal length for the last L values of the
  level; T_011 is longer than C1 only for n in {1, 2, 4}. The same holds for every unbordered 3-letter terminator.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C11.md#corollary-4-clm-c11-04--t_011-versus-the-fibonacci-code
- **evidence:** EXP-C11-06
- **novelty_basis:**

  Partial - T_011 is Capocelli's code C(3) (all words in which 011 occurs only as a suffix; IEEE Trans. IT 35 (1989) 191-193, as attributed by Lima-da Rocha, JCIS 2013, https://jcis.sbrt.org.br/jcis/article/download/17/11/127). T_p in general is the pattern code L(p): Lakshmanan, IEEE Trans. IT 27 (1981), in Fraenkel-Klein's version with p itself adjoined (DAM 64 (1996), section 2.1 and section 3); Berstel-Perrin call these semaphore codes. Lima-da Rocha (JCIS 2013, Table I) tabulate C(3) against the Fibonacci code at n = 2^t: longer at 1, 2, 4, equal at 8-32, one bit shorter for 2^6 <= n <= 2^26. The exact characterisation (one bit shorter except on the last L values of each level) was not found; Capocelli 1989 was not read (IEEE Xplore blocked), so it may be a full rediscovery (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **applications:** A drop-in prefix-free replacement for the Fibonacci code that saves one bit on almost every integer.
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-19T23:04:05Z novelty: POTENTIALLY NOVEL → REDISCOVERY (sync:C11; Historian audit 2026-10-19 (reviews/historian_C11_C20.md) - POTENTIALLY NOVEL -> REDISCOVERY (partial: Capocelli's code C(3), Lima-da Rocha's table))

### CLM-C11-05: H-C11-2: 1^k is the best length-k terminator beyond small n (refuted at practical sizes)

- **cycle:** C11
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** REFUTED

- **evidence:** EXP-C11-02

  k = 4, n = 1000: terminators 1000, 1001 and 1010 give codewords 1 bit shorter than 1111. Rivals beat 1^k at 762, 972 and 1127 sampled n >= 1000 (all n <= 2048 and quarter-powers of 2 up to 2^64) for k = 4, 5, 6, up to n = 311743, 2^45 and about 1.3e19 respectively (EXP-C11-02). By Theorem 2 (CLM-C11-02), 1^k does win eventually.


### CLM-C11-06: Average gain of an unbordered terminator over 1^k: log_lambda(1/(lambda-1)), then convergence to (2-lambda)/(lambda-1)^2 (both refuted)

- **cycle:** C11
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  (a) The average over n <= N of L_(1^k)(n) - L_u(n), u unbordered of length k + 1, tends to log_lambda(1/(lambda - 1)).
  (b) Corrected version: this average converges to (2 - lambda)/(lambda - 1)^2.

- **evidence:** EXP-C11-06; EXP-C11-07
- **counterexample:**

  k = 3 (111 vs 0111): the average over n <= 2^40 is 0.2282 against the prediction 0.2875 of (a) (EXP-C11-06); the running average is 0.228181, 0.309709 and 0.228548 at N = 2^40, 2^80 and 2^120, so it does not converge (EXP-C11-07). Both statements hold only for k = 2 (value 1).


### CLM-C11-07: The average gain oscillates between (2-lambda)/(lambda-1)^2 and lambda(2-lambda)/(lambda-1)

- **cycle:** C11
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C11.md Proposition 5), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); both constants agree to 6 digits with exact averages at L = 100, 200, 400 for k = 2..6 (EXP-C11-08)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For k >= 2, lambda the k-bonacci constant and u unbordered of length k + 1, the running average
  G(N) = (1/N) sum_(n<=N) (L_(1^k)(n) - L_u(n)) has liminf (2 - lambda)/(lambda - 1)^2 and limsup
  lambda(2 - lambda)/(lambda - 1) (both 1 for k = 2; 0.228155 and 0.352201 for k = 3).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C11.md#proposition-5-clm-c11-07--the-average-gain-oscillates

Their lengths are tabulated (Lima-da Rocha, JCIS 2013, Table I), but no analysis of the running average gain, its oscillation or the constants (2-lambda)/(lambda-1)^2, lambda(2-lambda)/(lambda-1) was found. Capocelli 1989 and Lakshmanan 1981 were not read (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

### CLM-C11-08: Fixed pattern-terminated codes beat classical universal codes on heavy-tailed and wide-range sources (mixed result)

- **cycle:** C11
- **type:** algorithmic
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **improvement:** MIXED
- **statement:**

  Among fixed (untuned) universal codes, a pattern-terminated code (T_101, T_0111 or T_00111) has the smallest
  expected length on 5 of 12 synthetic sources (Zipf 1.1, geometric 0.001, uniform 2^16, uniform 2^32,
  log-uniform on [1, 1e6]) and on both posting-list data sets, beating the best classical universal code
  (Elias, Levenshtein, Fibonacci, Fraenkel-Klein C3, LEB128, fixed Exp-Golomb) by 0.34-3.06 bits per value; on
  Zipf 1.1, log-uniform and the bursty posting list it also beats Golomb and Exp-Golomb codes tuned to the source.
  It loses on light-tailed sources (Zipf >= 1.5, geometric >= 0.01, prime gaps) and to tuned Golomb/Exp-Golomb
  codes on geometric and uniform sources, and the reference implementation is 4-10 times slower than the Elias
  and Exp-Golomb codes.

- **evidence:** EXP-C11-03; EXP-C11-04; EXP-C11-05; EXP-C11-09
- **applications:** Universal coding of heavy-tailed integer streams of unknown scale (posting-list gaps, magnitudes spread over many orders).
- **limitations:**

  12 synthetic sources (exact expectations) and 4 data sets, two of them simulated; memoryless codes compared with zeroth-order entropy; tuned codes are tuned on the evaluated data (optimistic for them); CPython string implementations without table-driven decoding; no claim of optimality beyond the tested baselines.


### CLM-C11-09: T_011 is never longer than Fraenkel-Klein's C3 except at n = 2, 4, 8; C3 is never longer than C1 except at n = 1

- **cycle:** C11
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C11.md Corollary 6, from cum_011(L) - cum_C3(L) = F_(L-1) - (L - 1)), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); exhaustive check for n <= 2e6 and the count inequalities for L < 300 (EXP-C11-09)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The Fraenkel-Klein code C3 (2F_(r-2) codewords of length r >= 3, so cum_C3(L) = 2F_L - 2) is never longer than the
  Fibonacci code C1 except at n = 1, and T_011 is never longer than C3 except at n in {2, 4, 8}; moreover
  cum_011(L)/cum_C3(L) -> phi^2/2 and cum_C3(L)/cum_C1(L) -> 2/phi.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C11.md#corollary-6-clm-c11-09--t_011-versus-the-fraenkelklein-code-c3
- **evidence:** EXP-C11-09
- **novelty_basis:**

  Partial - the count 2F_(r-2) and C3 <= C1 for n > 1 are Fraenkel-Klein (DAM 64 (1996), Prop. 5 and the proof of Prop. 6, https://u.cs.biu.ac.il/~tomi/Postscripts/robust.pdf). The comparison of C3 with T_011 (= Capocelli's C(3), 1989), which is longer only at n = 2, 4, 8, was not found: Fraenkel-Klein do not cite Capocelli, and Lima-da Rocha (2013) do not treat C3 (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-19T23:04:05Z novelty: POTENTIALLY NOVEL → REDISCOVERY (sync:C11; Historian audit 2026-10-19 (reviews/historian_C11_C20.md) - POTENTIALLY NOVEL -> REDISCOVERY (partial: Fraenkel-Klein Props 5-6))

### CLM-C12-01: Divisibility games: subtract a proper divisor = v2, subtract a divisor = v2 + 1, move to a divisor = Omega

- **cycle:** C12
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C12.md Theorem 1), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); 0 mismatches for n <= 1e5 (EXP-C12-02)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  G01 (n -> n - d with d | n, d < n) has Grundy function g(n) = v2(n) (n >= 1); G02 (d = n also allowed) has
  g(n) = v2(n) + 1 (n >= 1); G04 (n -> d with d | n, d < n) has g(n) = Omega(n).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C12.md#theorem-1-clm-c12-01--divisibility-games
- **evidence:** EXP-C12-01; EXP-C12-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Iannucci & Larsson, "Game values of arithmetic functions" (arXiv:2101.07608, 2021), Theorem 1 (maliquot =
  G04) and Theorem 2 (saliquot = G02); G01 is the "Divisor Game" puzzle (LeetCode 1025) and its Grundy
  function follows from the saliquot proof. OEIS A007814, A001511, A001222.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C12-02: Coprimality games: index of the smallest prime factor; the non-coprime game has g(2k) = k - 1

- **cycle:** C12
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C12.md Theorem 2), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); 0 mismatches for n <= 5000 (G07 = G05 for n <= 1500) (EXP-C12-02)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  G05 (n -> m, 1 <= m < n, gcd(m, n) = 1), which coincides with "subtract k, 1 <= k < n, gcd(k, n) = 1", has
  g(1) = 0 and g(n) = j for n >= 2, where the smallest prime factor of n is the j-th prime. G06 (n -> m,
  1 <= m < n, gcd(m, n) > 1) has g(2k) = k - 1, g(m) <= (m - 1)/2 for odd m, and its P-positions n >= 1 are
  exactly 1 and the primes.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C12.md#theorem-2-clm-c12-02--coprimality-games
- **evidence:** EXP-C12-01; EXP-C12-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Iannucci & Larsson 2021, Theorem 5 (totative = G05, with the subtraction equivalence) and Theorem 6
  (nontotative: SG(2n) = n, SG(n) = 1 iff n prime, i.e. our G06 values plus one; they also prove SG(n) <= n/2).
  OEIS A055396, A279119 (= G06 exactly), A008578.

- **limitations:** No closed form is known to us for the odd values of G06; g(m)/m < 0.24985 for odd m <= 5000 (a bound m/4 is not proved).
- **status history:**

### CLM-C12-03: P-positions of the deleting-divisors game (subtract d | n with 1 < d < n): 0, odd numbers, 2^(2j+1)

- **cycle:** C12
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib: Infinity.C12.g03_pset and Infinity.C12.g03_P_positions (axioms propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound; no sorry, no native_decide); 0 mismatches for n <= 1e5 (EXP-C12-02)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  In G03 (n -> n - d with d | n and 1 < d < n) the P-positions are exactly 0, the odd numbers and the powers
  2^(2j+1).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C12.md#theorem-3-clm-c12-03--p-positions-of-the-deleting-divisors-game
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C12_Games.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C12-02; EXP-C12-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  Codeforces problem 1537D "Deleting Divisors" (Round #726, June 2021): the official solution declares the
  second player the winner exactly for odd n and for n = 2^k with k odd. First assessed as potentially novel
  (no OEIS match); downgraded after the Historian found the problem.


### CLM-C12-04: Grundy function of the deleting-divisors game: v2(n) except on odd parts <= 15

- **cycle:** C12
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Computer-assisted (proofs/paper/C12.md Theorem 8): exact computation for n <= 1e5 and an inductive-step
  certificate for odd parts <= 15 and 13 <= a <= 100 (EXP-C12-06, 0 failures); written induction for odd parts
  >= 17 and shift-invariance argument for odd parts <= 15; odd n via the Lean P-set theorem g03_pset. Trust
  base: exact Python integer arithmetic plus the written Steps 1-3, skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18
  (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md, A.5 - steps correct; the Skeptic's own C computation gives g = the closed form
  for all n <= 1e8, the mex certificate for 6 <= a <= 400 and the shift invariance for 13 <= a <= 400).

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  Let g be the Grundy function of G03 and n = 2^a m with m odd. Then g(n) = 0 for odd n, and for a >= 1:
  g(2^a m) = a if m >= 17; g(2^a) = 0 for odd a and a - 1 for even a, except g(16) = 2; for m = 3 the values
  are 2, 3, 4, 3, 6 (a = 1..5) and a - 2 (a >= 6); for m = 5 they are 1, 3, 4, 3, 6 (a = 1..5) and a + 2
  (a >= 6); for m = 7, 9, 11, 15 they are a (a <= 2) and a + 1 (a >= 3); for m = 13 they are a (a <= 3) and
  a + 1 (a >= 4). Consequently |g(n) - v2(n)| <= 2 unless n is an odd power of 2, and the record values occur
  at n = 5 * 2^a for a >= 6.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C12.md#theorem-8-clm-c12-04--grundy-values-of-the-deleting-divisors-game
- **evidence:** EXP-C12-06; EXP-C12-04; EXP-C12-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Not in Iannucci-Larsson (arXiv:2101.07608), whose divisor-subtraction game allows d = 1 and d = n; Codeforces 1537D and its solution give only the P-positions. OEIS has neither the Grundy sequence nor the P-position sequence (queried 2026-10-18 and again 2026-10-19). Web searches found no analysis of the Grundy values. Not a priority claim (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **applications:** Sums of deleting-divisors heaps are solved by XOR of closed-form values (the P-positions alone do not solve sums).
- **limitations:** Beyond a = 100 (the Skeptic - a = 400) the certificate relies on the written shift-invariance argument.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C12-05: Bitwise and quotient games: popcount, floor(log2((n+1)/3)) + 1 and floor(log2 n)

- **cycle:** C12
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C12.md Theorem 4), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); 0 mismatches for n <= 1e5, 5000 and 2e4 respectively (EXP-C12-02)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  G13 (move to a proper binary submask) has g(n) = popcount(n); G19 (n -> n mod k, 1 <= k < n) has
  g(n) = floor(log2((n + 1)/3)) + 1 for n >= 2 (g(0) = g(1) = 0); G20 (n -> floor(n/k), 2 <= k <= n) has
  g(n) = floor(log2 n) for n >= 1.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C12.md#theorem-4-clm-c12-05--bitwise-and-quotient-games
- **evidence:** EXP-C12-01; EXP-C12-02; EXP-C12-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  G19 is the residue-throw-divisor game, Theorem 12 of Iannucci & Larsson 2021 (proof left to the reader there;
  our written proof supplies it). Sequences OEIS A000120, A099396 (g(n) = A099396(n + 1)), A000523. G13 is Nim
  on the number of 1-bits; the quotient game G20 was not located in the literature but its analysis is
  elementary.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C12-06: Residue lemma; subtract-a-power-of-k has the Grundy function of the subtraction game {1, k}

- **cycle:** C12
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  (a) machine-checked in Lean (Infinity.C12.residue_grundy, Infinity.C12.power_of_two_grundy; axioms propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound; no sorry); (b) written proof (proofs/paper/C12.md Theorem 5), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); 0 mismatches for k = 2..20, n <= 2e4 (EXP-C12-02)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  (a) If no element of the move set S is divisible by q and 1, ..., q - 1 are in S, the subtraction game S has
  g(n) = n mod q; in particular subtract-a-power-of-2 has g(n) = n mod 3. (b) For every k >= 2,
  subtract-a-power-of-k has the same Grundy function as the subtraction game {1, k}: n mod 2 for odd k, and
  P(n mod (k + 1)) with P(r) = r mod 2 for r < k and P(k) = 2 for even k.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C12.md#theorem-5-clm-c12-06--the-residue-lemma-and-powers-of-k
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C12_Games.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C12-01; EXP-C12-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Two-element subtraction games and their periods are classical (Berlekamp, Conway & Guy, Winning Ways);
  subtract-a-power-of-2 = n mod 3 is folklore (Grundy sequence OEIS A010872). The reduction of powers of k to
  {1, k} is a one-line congruence (k^i = +-1 mod k + 1); it was not located verbatim.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C12-07: Splitting games in which every heap can be split have g(n) = [n even]

- **cycle:** C12
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib: Infinity.C12.split_parity (axioms propext, Quot.sound; no sorry); G16, G17 and 40 random splitting relations checked (EXP-C12-02)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  In every take-and-break game in which a heap is replaced by two nonempty heaps from a set of allowed splits,
  and every heap n >= 2 has at least one allowed split, g(n) = 1 for even n and g(n) = 0 for odd n (n >= 1);
  equivalently, every play lasts exactly n - 1 moves.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C12.md#theorem-6-clm-c12-07--splitting-games
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C12_Games.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C12-01; EXP-C12-02
- **novelty_basis:** Classical parity argument (every move adds one heap, as in the chocolate-bar puzzle); the sequences are OEIS A000035 / A059841.

### CLM-C12-08: Subtract-a-square and subtract-a-triangular-number have infinite density-zero P-sets and are not periodic

- **cycle:** C12
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C12.md Theorem 7) from the Furstenberg-Sarkozy theorem and its polynomial version (Kamae-Mendes France 1978), which are cited, not re-proved; skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C05_C08_C12.md); P-densities 0.0222 and 0.0116 at n <= 2e5 (EXP-C12-03)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For the subtraction games whose move sets are the positive squares and the triangular numbers, the set of
  P-positions is infinite and has natural density 0; hence neither Grundy sequence is eventually periodic or
  eventually arithmetic-periodic.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C12.md#theorem-7-clm-c12-08--sparse-sets-with-density-zero-p-positions
- **evidence:** EXP-C12-03; EXP-C12-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  For squares the P-set is a maximal square-difference-free set, of density zero by Furstenberg-Sarkozy
  (Wikipedia "Square-difference-free set"; D. Eppstein, "Faster evaluation of subtraction games", FUN 2018).
  The triangular case is the same argument with an intersective polynomial.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C12-09: H-C12-0: every relational game is periodic, arithmetic-periodic or has a closed form (inconclusive)

- **cycle:** C12
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** INCONCLUSIVE
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  Every game of the catalogue is eventually periodic, eventually arithmetic-periodic, or has a Grundy function
  given by a closed form in a number-theoretic function.

- **evidence:** EXP-C12-01; EXP-C12-02; EXP-C12-06; EXP-C12-03
- **limitations:** The "closed form" clause is not precisely defined, so the hypothesis cannot be refuted as stated; recorded as inconclusive rather than refuted.
- **notes:**

  13 of 19 games conform (5 periodic; closed forms for G01-G05, G13, G19, G20, G03 by CLM-C12-04).
  Subtract-a-square and subtract-a-triangular-number are provably neither eventually periodic nor
  arithmetic-periodic (CLM-C12-08), but the absence of a closed form is not a theorem. G06 (odd values),
  subtract-a-Fibonacci, subtract-a-prime and Grundy's game remain unexplained within range (whether Grundy's
  game is eventually periodic is a well-known open problem).


### CLM-C12-10: H-C12-1: Grundy values of divisibility/coprimality games depend only on the prime signature (refuted)

- **cycle:** C12
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For each of the games G01-G06, g(n) depends only on the prime signature (sorted exponent vector) of n.
- **evidence:** EXP-C12-05
- **counterexample:**

  G01: n = 2 and n = 3 have signature (1) but g = 1 and 0. Further first counterexamples: G02 (2, 3: g = 2, 1),
  G03 (4, 9: 1, 0), G05 (2, 3: 1, 2), G06 (6, 10: 2, 4). Only G04 (g = Omega) depends on the signature alone.

### CLM-C13-01: Affine anchored spectra are root sets of polynomial systems - divisor-closed, CRT-closed, finite ones are divisor sets

- **cycle:** C13
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 (complete, except the standard fact quoted as an example that a resultant of two polynomials lies in the ideal they generate) - proofs/lean/Infinity/C13_AffineStructure.lean: the anchored affine tables are exactly x + a*y + 1 (affine_anchored, affine_table_anchored); a law holds in x + a*y + 1 on Z/N iff a is a common root mod N of its affine system (law_holds_affine_iff, affModels_eq); closure under divisors and coprime products (aff_of_dvd, aff_mul_of_coprime, mem_aff_iff_primePow); with no common factor over Q the spectrum is finite, equal to the divisors of its maximum L, and L divides every integer of the ideal (aff_structure_of_no_common_factor); a common factor of positive degree makes it infinite (aff_infinite - via Gauss's lemma instead of the paper's Schur argument) and the empty system gives every N (aff_empty); Theorem 1(f) as well (aff_of_root, primePow_root_iff, primePow_mem_aff_iff_of_coprime_cofactors); standard axioms only. The paper proof was skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C13_C19.md); consistent with all 152 spectra of EXP-C13-01

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For any finite set E of laws, the anchored affine tables are exactly x*y = x + a*y + 1, and the affine spectrum Aff(E) is the set of N for which the affine system of E (polynomials in Z[a]) has a common root mod N. Aff(E) is closed under divisors and under coprime products; if the system has no common factor over Q it is finite and equal to the set of divisors of its maximum L (L divides every non-zero integer of the ideal generated by the system in Z[a], e.g. any non-zero constant of the system or any non-zero resultant of two of its polynomials), and otherwise it is infinite (an empty system gives Aff(E) = N).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C13.md#theorem-1-clm-c13-01--the-structure-of-affine-spectra
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C13_AffineStructure.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C13-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Elementary consequences of the CRT and of classical facts on polynomial congruences; the reduction of identities to conditions on affine (linear) representations is standard for quasigroups and medial magmas (Toyoda-Bruck). Recorded as the lab's tool, not as new mathematics. The Equational Theories Project also studies spectra of single magma laws with linear models x * y = ax + by mod n (Bolan et al., arXiv:2512.07087; noted by the Skeptic from the abstract page).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C13-02: Right-normed laws (xy)z = x V(y,z) have only translation models

- **cycle:** C13
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib (translation_form, right_normed_translation - stated for an arbitrary binary function V, so more general than the English; standard axioms only); the relation k(V(y,z)) = k(y) + k(z) + 1 by the written proof (one substitution, proofs/paper/C13.md Theorem 2)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For every term V(y, z) not containing x, every anchored model of (x*y)*z = x*V(y, z) is a translation table x*y = x + 1 + k(y) with k a permutation of Z/N fixing 0, and k satisfies k(V(y,z)) = k(y) + k(z) + 1.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C13.md#theorem-2-clm-c13-02--right-normed-laws-have-only-translation-models
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C13_Spectra.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C13-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence. Anchored magmas impose Laver's initial condition x * 0 = x + 1 on arbitrary laws. Spectra of single magma laws are studied without anchor in the Equational Theories Project (arXiv:2512.07087 section 9; B. Le Floch, 'Spectra of equational laws', 2025). No treatment of anchored models or of right-normed laws was found. Small lemma (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C13-03: The W-family: anchored models of (xy)z = x(y W(z)) are the affine tables with a*c_W(a) = 1

- **cycle:** C13
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib for the model correspondence for every W (w_family_models, w_family_model_of_root, linear_of_shift, T1.eval_affine, T1.cpoly_eq; standard axioms only); the characterisation of the occurring c_W by written proof (proofs/paper/C13.md Theorem 3 (iv), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C13_C19.md); the Skeptic confirmed (iv) for all trees with up to 16 leaves); 0 mismatches on 23 terms for N <= 9 (EXP-C13-03)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For every one-variable term W(z), the anchored models of (x*y)*z = x*(y*W(z)) are exactly the affine tables
  x*y = x + 1 + a*y with a*c_W(a) = 1 (mod N), c_W(a) the sum over the leaves of W of a^(number of right steps);
  distinct roots give distinct models. The occurring polynomials c_W are exactly those with non-negative integer
  coefficients, constant term 1 and no gaps. In particular the right comb with n leaves gives the spectrum of the
  n-bonacci polynomial (golden law for n = 2, tribonacci law for n = 3) and the left comb with k + 1 leaves the
  condition k*a^2 + a - 1 = 0.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C13.md#theorem-3-clm-c13-03--the-w-family-polynomial-spectra-apa--1
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C13_Spectra.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C13-03; EXP-C13-09
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence. Reducing laws to polynomial congruences through linear models is standard (Toyoda-Bruck; the ETP uses x * y = ax + by over Z/n, arXiv:2512.07087 section 9). The anchored W-family theorem was not found: true spectrum = roots of a*c_W(a) - 1, and every gap-free c_W with constant term 1 occurs. The number theory of the resulting sets is classical (earlier searches: Klaska-Skula, Fibonacci Quart. 2010; Moree-Noubissie arXiv:2205.06685) (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

  Settles two cases of the lab's own conjecture CLM-C06-10; anchored spectra do not occur in the ETP material read (arXiv:2512.07087 section 9; Le Floch 2025). Small (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

### CLM-C13-05: The law x = x(x(xx)) has anchored models of every size except 2

- **cycle:** C13
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib (cube_law_no_two, cubeOp_model with an explicit model for every N >= 3; standard axioms only); written proof proofs/paper/C13.md Theorem 5; explicit models re-checked numerically for N = 3..60 (EXP-C13-09)

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  The one-variable law x = x*(x*(x*x)) has an anchored model of every size N != 2 and none of size 2; in particular its spectrum contains 4 but not 2, so anchored spectra need not be closed under divisors.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C13.md#theorem-5-clm-c13-05--a-spectrum-that-is-not-closed-under-divisors
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C13_Spectra.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C13-07; EXP-C13-09
- **novelty_basis:** Elementary; its interest is as the counterexample to the lab's hypothesis H-C13-1.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C13-06: Offset congruence closure is a sound prover of finite anchored spectra; it settles 16 of the 17 laws of CLM-C06-10

- **cycle:** C13
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written soundness proof (proofs/paper/C13.md Theorem 6); applications computer-assisted (Python, EXP-C13-08) - trust base is the closure code, cross-checked against SAT/affine models on 603 laws (235 with a bound, 1,245 sizes) with 0 contradictions; skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C13_C19.md) - its independent closure proves the same 16 laws and 6,912 exact spectra, with 0 contradictions against all anchored tables at N = 2, 3 and 28,890 SAT instances at N = 4-7

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  The procedure offset_closure_bound (systems/anchored.py) returns g >= 0 such that N | g for every anchored model of
  size N. If g > 0 and every divisor of g admits an affine model, the anchored spectrum is exactly Div(g). This proves
  the conjectured spectra {1,3} resp. {1,2} of CLM-C06-10 for all its laws except x(yz) = y((yx)z), and the exact
  finite spectrum Div(g) for 6,912 of the 18,581 laws over {x,y,z} with <= 4 leaves per side (6,045 of them have
  only the trivial model).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C13.md#theorem-6-clm-c13-06--soundness-of-offset-congruence-closure
- **evidence:** EXP-C13-08; EXP-C13-04
- **novelty_basis:** Congruence closure with integer offsets is a standard decision-procedure idea (difference/offset equalities, Nelson-Oppen style); its use as a spectrum prover for anchored magmas is the lab's.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C13-07: H-C13-1: every anchored spectrum is closed under divisors (refuted)

- **cycle:** C13
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** If a set of laws has an anchored model of size N, it has one of every size d dividing N.
- **evidence:** EXP-C13-05; EXP-C13-06; EXP-C13-07
- **counterexample:**

  x = x(x(xx)) has models of every size except 2 (CLM-C13-05, Lean). Violations are common - 24 of 253 one-variable laws (N <= 12), 81 of 300 random 2-variable laws (N <= 9), 29 of 150 random 3-variable laws and 17 of 80 random pairs of laws (N <= 8); e.g. ((xy)x)y = (y(xx))x has models at N = 6 and 8 but none at N = 2, 3, 4, 5.


### CLM-C13-08: H-C13-2: laws with an infinite non-trivial affine spectrum are affinely rigid (refuted)

- **cycle:** C13
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For every law with at most 4 leaves per side (3 variables) or 5 leaves per side (2 variables) whose affine spectrum is infinite and not all N, every anchored model is affine.
- **evidence:** EXP-C13-02
- **counterexample:**

  146 of the 147 laws tested from infinite non-trivial affine classes have non-affine models (EXP-C13-02, corrected run), all at every N = 6, 7, 8; e.g. x = x(x(xx)) (affine spectrum A034017, non-affine models for N = 3..8) and (xx)x = (yy)y (non-affine models for N = 2..8). (The first run also tested 52 laws from finite classes Div(L), L >= 61, through a faulty filter - they are all non-rigid too.)

### CLM-C13-09: H-C13-5: a one-variable law has spectrum {1} or a cofinite spectrum (refuted)

- **cycle:** C13
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED

  x = (xx)x has spectrum exactly {1, 2}: at x = 0 the anchor gives 0 = 2, and x*y = x + 1 is a model of size 2; likewise x = ((xx)x)x has spectrum {1, 3} and x = (((xx)x)x)x has {1, 2, 4} (proofs/paper/C13.md, remark after Theorem 5).

The Skeptic extended the check to all 2,080 one-variable laws with <= 6 leaves (N <= 12) - spectra {1}, all N, Div(2), Div(3), Div(4), Div(5), or all N except {2}, {3}, {2,3}, {2,4}, {4} or {2,3,4}; nothing neither finite nor cofinite.

### CLM-C13-11: Single laws whose affine spectra are classical arithmetic sets

- **cycle:** C13
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  affine systems computed mechanically (EXP-C13-01) and Theorem 1 (written); the identification of each root set with the stated arithmetic condition is elementary (e.g. a^2 - 2a - 1 = 0 iff (a - 1)^2 = 2); terms <= 200 agree with the OEIS entries A005408, A001651, A008784, A057126, A057125, A034017, A089270, A004613, A058529, A192453, A354467; Theorem 1 (f) added after the Skeptic's review (the first statement cited exceptional primes that Theorem 1 did not contain); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C13_C19.md) - all 11 identifications equal the arithmetic definitions for every N <= 2000

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  The affine anchored spectrum of a law contains {N : G has a root mod N}, G the primitive Q-gcd of its affine system, with equality at every prime not dividing a non-zero integer of the ideal generated by the cofactors f/G (Theorem 1 (f)); for the eleven polynomials below the two sets coincide for every N, because each is realised by a law whose system contains G itself (e.g. x(xx) = y(xy) has system {a^2 + 1}, (xx)(xy) = (yy)(yx) has {a^2 - 2a - 1}, ((xx)x)x = x((xx)(xx)) has {a^2 + 2a - 2, a^3 + 2a^2 - 2a}). Among laws with at most 4 leaves (3 variables) or 5 leaves (2 variables) this realises - via the polynomials 2a + 1, 3a + 1, a^2 + 1, a^2 - 2a - 1, a^2 + 2a - 2, a^2 + a + 1, a^2 - a - 1, 2a^2 + 2a + 1, 2a^2 - 1, a^4 + 1, a^4 - a^2 + 1 - the odd numbers, the numbers prime to 3, and the numbers N modulo which -1, 2, 3 are squares, x^2 + x + 1 has a root, the golden ratio exists, all prime factors are 1 mod 4, +-1 mod 8 or 1 mod 12, and -1 is a fourth power.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C13.md#theorem-1-clm-c13-01--the-structure-of-affine-spectra
- **evidence:** EXP-C13-01; EXP-C13-09
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence. Classical arithmetic sets occur as (unanchored) spectra of single laws in the Equational Theories Project (e.g. law 546: sums of two squares; Le Floch's notes section 2.1). Their realisation as affine anchored spectra was not found. The sets themselves are classical (OEIS entries quoted). These are affine spectra only - by EXP-C13-02 the true spectra of the short laws realising them are usually larger (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

- **cycle:** C13

- **evidence:** EXP-C13-02; EXP-C13-05; EXP-C13-06

### CLM-C14-01: Associativity of a local graph product is a finite identity on relations, detected by P3 x P3 x P3 (Lean)

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (rel_lprod, assoc_iso, assoc_iso_nonempty, assoc_of_P3; arbitrary vertex types; standard axioms only); commutative part written
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  For a local product S the canonical bijection ((g,h),k) -> (g,(h,k)) is an isomorphism (G*H)*K -> G*(H*K)
  for all simple graphs G, H, K iff it is one for G = H = K = P3 iff phi_S(phi_S(r1,r2),r3) =
  phi_S(r1,phi_S(r2,r3)) for all relations r1, r2, r3 in {E, A, N}. Likewise the swap is an isomorphism
  G*H -> H*G for all G, H iff S is symmetric.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C14.md#theorem-1-clm-c14-01--associativity-is-a-finite-identity-on-relations
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C14_GraphProducts.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C14-01; EXP-C14-06
- **novelty_basis:** Elementary and implicit in the Imrich-Izbicki (1975) scheme of products defined by factor adjacencies; the full text of that paper was not consulted.

### CLM-C14-02: Exactly 20 of the 256 local graph products are associative, 10 commutative, 8 with unit K1 (Lean decide)

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 kernel computation (assoc_codes, comm_assoc_codes by decide; standard axioms) on top of the Lean-verified reduction CLM-C14-01; independently in two Python implementations with graph-level brute force (EXP-C14-01, EXP-C14-06)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The associative local products are the codes 0, 1, 4, 5, 8, 13, 28, 29, 31, 73, 77, 85, 93, 109, 127, 141,
  221, 223, 253, 255 (bit order EA, EN, AE, AA, AN, NE, NA, NN). The commutative ones among them are empty (0),
  Cartesian (5), direct (8), strong (13), symmetric difference (85), co-normal (93), 127, 141, 221 and complete
  (255). Exactly 8 have the two-sided unit K1: Cartesian, strong, lexicographic (29), its transpose (77),
  symmetric difference, co-normal, 141, 221. They form 8 orbits under transposition and complement-conjugation.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C14.md#theorem-2-clm-c14-02--the-twenty-associative-products
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C14_GraphProducts.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C14-01; EXP-C14-06
- **novelty_basis:**

  W. Imrich, H. Izbicki, Associative products of graphs, Monatsh. Math. 80 (1975) 277-281, doi:10.1007/BF01472575 - exactly twenty associative products; R. J. Nowakowski, D. F. Rall, Discuss. Math. Graph Theory 16 (1996) 53-79, doi:10.7151/dmgt.1023 - ten depend on both factors, eight of those commutative. Our list agrees with both counts.

- **references:** https://pure.unileoben.ac.at/en/publications/associative-products-of-graphs/; https://www.dmgt.uz.zgora.pl/publish/article.php?doi=1023

### CLM-C14-03: For local graph products, associativity and commutativity up to isomorphism are no weaker than the strict notions

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  computer-assisted - explicit witnesses for all 236 + 224 codes, each certified by edge count/degree sequence (hand-checkable), found in EXP-C14-02 and re-certified by the independent implementation of EXP-C14-06

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  A local product satisfies (G*H)*K isomorphic to G*(H*K) for all graphs iff it is strictly associative, and
  G*H isomorphic to H*G for all graphs iff S is symmetric. For each of the 236 non-associative (224
  non-commutative) products a witness exists with factors of at most 3 vertices whose two sides differ in edge
  count or degree sequence.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C14.md#theorem-3-clm-c14-03--up-to-isomorphism-is-no-weaker
- **evidence:** EXP-C14-02; EXP-C14-06
- **novelty_basis:** Bold hypothesis H-C14-1 of this cycle; whether Imrich-Izbicki or later work address isomorphism-level associativity could not be checked (abstracts only).

### CLM-C14-04: Exactly six local products are Kronecker products in disguise (multiplicative characters)

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written case analysis (proofs/paper/C14.md Theorem 4); polynomial system solved over C by sympy for all 256 codes (EXP-C14-03); Lean kernel computation for values in {-1,0,1} (character_codes); numerical matrix checks on random graphs; skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  There is c = (cE, cA, cN) in C^3 with cA != cN and M_c(G*H) = M_c(G) (x) M_c(H) for all graphs, where
  M_c(G) = cE I + cA A + cN (J - I - A), exactly for: direct (c = (0,1,0), M = A), code 127 (0,0,1),
  strong (1,1,0), co-normal (1,0,1), symmetric difference (1,-1,1), M = S(G) + I with S the Seidel matrix,
  and code 141 (1,1,-1), M = I - S(G); the character is unique in each case, and every such product is
  associative and commutative.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C14.md#theorem-4-clm-c14-04--multiplicative-characters
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C14_GraphProducts.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C14-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  The individual identities for the direct (A (x) B) and strong ((A+I) (x) (B+I)) products are classical and the co-normal one follows by complementation; the symmetric-difference case is in Abiad-Butler-Haemers (arXiv:1801.01149, Section 5; see CLM-C14-06). A classification of the local products with such a linearisation was not found. Small result; possibly folklore. Searches on Boolean/NEPS products and Seidel spectra (2026-10-19) found no classification; Cvetkovic-Doob-Sachs, Spectra of Graphs, sections 2.5-2.6 not checked. Possibly folklore (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C14-05: Additive characters: only the Cartesian product and its complement-dual; every non-trivial commutative associative product has a character

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C14.md Theorem 5); sympy over C for all 256 codes (EXP-C14-03); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  M_c(G*H) = M_c(G) (x) I + I (x) M_c(H) for all graphs (cA != cN) holds exactly for the Cartesian product
  (c = (0,t,0)) and code 221 (c = (0,0,t)), t != 0. Hence each of the ten commutative associative local
  products except the empty and the complete product has exactly one kind of character (multiplicative or
  additive).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C14.md#theorem-5-clm-c14-05--additive-characters-every-non-trivial-commutative-associative-product-has-a-character
- **evidence:** EXP-C14-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  The Kronecker-sum formula for the Cartesian product is classical; the classification over all 256 local products was not found (same searches as CLM-C14-04). Small result. Searches on Boolean/NEPS products and Seidel spectra (2026-10-19) found no classification; Cvetkovic-Doob-Sachs, Spectra of Graphs, sections 2.5-2.6 not checked. Possibly folklore (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

### CLM-C14-06: The symmetric-difference product is the Kronecker product of Seidel matrices; it acts on two-graphs

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C14.md Theorem 6); entry-level identity in Lean (xor_character); exact integer check for all pairs of graphs with <= 3 vertices (EXP-C14-06); spectra on 30 random pairs (EXP-C14-03); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For all graphs G, H: S(G xor H) + I = (S(G) + I) (x) (S(H) + I), where xor is the symmetric-difference product
  and S the Seidel matrix. Hence the Seidel eigenvalues of G xor H are (theta+1)(eta+1) - 1, and xor is
  compatible with Seidel switching, giving a commutative associative product on two-graphs with unit [K1].

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C14.md#theorem-6-clm-c14-06--the-symmetric-difference-is-the-kronecker-product-of-seidel-matrices
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C14_GraphProducts.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C14-03; EXP-C14-06
- **novelty_basis:**

  A. Abiad, S. Butler and W. H. Haemers, arXiv:1801.01149 (2018), Section 5 'A graph product and its 2-rank behaviour', define this product (their F2 formula A1 (x) J + J (x) A2 is the symmetric-difference product; their verbal definition inverts the adjacency rule) and state that its Seidel matrix is (S1 + I) (x) (S2 + I) - I; their proof of Thm 5.1 uses its compatibility with Seidel switching. Found by the Skeptic - the lab's four searches missed it. The product itself is Harary-Wilcox (1967).

- **references:** https://www.mscand.dk/article/view/10817; https://www.themoonlight.io/review/some-new-results-on-seidel-equienergetic-graphs; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-graph
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-19T14:24:53Z novelty: POTENTIALLY NOVEL → REDISCOVERY (sync:C14; Skeptic review 2026-10-18 (P6) - the identity is published (Abiad-Butler-Haemers 2018); POTENTIALLY NOVEL -> REDISCOVERY)

### CLM-C14-07: Complete bipartite graphs multiply as (order, imbalance); K2 absorbs Seidel switching

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C14.md Theorem 7; (c) via twin reducts of +-1 matrices); (a) checked edge by edge for a,b,c,d <= 4, (b) on 200 random cases (EXP-C14-06); (c) on 12,713 pairs with <= 6 vertices (EXP-C14-05); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  (a) K_{a,b} xor K_{c,d} = K_{ac+bd, ad+bc}; so (|V|, |a-b|) is multiplicative on complete bipartite graphs.
  (b) K_{a,a} xor X is isomorphic to K_{a,a} xor X^Z for every switching X^Z of X (a >= 1).
  (c) K2 xor X is isomorphic to K2 xor Y iff X and Y are switching-equivalent.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C14.md#theorem-7-clm-c14-07--complete-bipartite-graphs-and-k-absorbs-switching
- **evidence:** EXP-C14-05; EXP-C14-06
- **novelty_basis:**

  Partial - (c) and (b) for a = 1 are Oh-Yoo-Yun, 'Rainbow graphs and switching classes', SIAM J. Discrete Math. 27 (2013) 1106-1111, Thm 1.1 and Prop. 3.3 (https://arxiv.org/abs/1108.6143). Their 2n-vertex graph G~ is K_2 (+) G, and G~ = H~ iff G and H are switching-equivalent; they note a similar map in Colbourn-Corneil 1980. (b) for a >= 2 follows since K_(a,a) = K_2 (+) aK_1. Part (a), K_(a,b) (+) K_(c,d) = K_(ac+bd, ad+bc), was not found (elementary) (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **references:** https://arxiv.org/abs/1108.6143
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-19T23:04:05Z novelty: POTENTIALLY NOVEL → REDISCOVERY (sync:C14; Historian audit 2026-10-19 (reviews/historian_C11_C20.md) - POTENTIALLY NOVEL -> REDISCOVERY (partial: Oh-Yoo-Yun 2013 prove (b) for a = 1 and (c)))

### CLM-C14-08: H-C14-3: the six commutative unital associative products factor uniquely on connected (or co-connected) graphs (refuted)

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  Each of the six commutative associative local products with unit K1 (Cartesian, strong, symmetric
  difference, and their complement-duals 221, co-normal, 141) has unique prime factorisation on connected
  graphs, or, for the complement-duals, on co-connected graphs.

- **evidence:** EXP-C14-04; EXP-C14-06
- **counterexample:**

  Symmetric difference: K2 xor K2 xor K2 is isomorphic to K2 xor K_{1,3}; K2 and K_{1,3} are connected and prime, so this connected graph of order 8 has the two factorisations {K2,K2,K2} and {K2,K_{1,3}} (proofs/paper/C14.md Theorem 8; explicit isomorphism in EXP-C14-06). In all graphs already C4 = K2 xor K2 = K2 xor 2K1. The other five products show no collision up to order 15 in the stated classes, consistent with Sabidussi-Vizing and Doerfler-Imrich/McKenzie.


### CLM-C14-09: The Cartesian and strong product monoids of all finite graphs are isomorphic graded monoids; equal prime counts (A129582)

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C14.md Theorem 9) relying on the published unique factorisation theorems for connected graphs (Sabidussi-Vizing; Doerfler-Imrich/McKenzie); census confirms equal counts for n <= 15 and reproduces A129582 to n = 15; skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  There is an order-preserving monoid isomorphism between (finite graphs, Cartesian product) and (finite graphs,
  strong product), and by complementation between the co-normal product and code 221; for every n the four
  products have the same number of prime graphs of order n, and the connected Cartesian and strong primes are
  equinumerous (OEIS A129582).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C14.md#theorem-9-clm-c14-09--cartesian-and-strong-products-give-isomorphic-graded-monoids
- **evidence:** EXP-C14-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  Connected Cartesian primes = OEIS A129582 (Ji Li, Prime graphs and exponential composition of species, J. Combin. Theory A 115 (2008) 1374-1401, arXiv:0705.0038). The semiring view of disconnected graphs and the parallel cancellation theory for Cartesian and strong products are in Hammack-Imrich-Klavzar's Handbook and Klavzar et al., Cancellation properties of products of graphs; the isomorphism statement is a reformulation.

- **references:** https://arxiv.org/abs/0705.0038; https://users.fmf.uni-lj.si/klavzar/preprints/CC.pdf
- **status history:**
