  2, 4, 8, 34, 148, 1044, 12326, 274658, 12005100, 1018997864; symmetric difference = code 141:
  2, 4, 9, 34, 150, 1044, 12333, 274658, 12005127, 1018997864; lexicographic = its transpose:
  2, 4, 7, 34, 142, 1044, 12310, 274652, 12005036, 1018997864 (values to n = 15 in results/EXP-C14-04.json).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C14.md#theorem-10-clm-c14-10--prime-counts-over-all-graphs-computer-assisted
- **evidence:** EXP-C14-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence. OEIS searches (2026-10-18, repeated 2026-10-19) found none of the three sequences; derived sequences of a natural kind, novelty unconfirmed (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C14-11: The lexicographic product has non-unique factorisation already at order 10 (different multisets of primes)

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** by hand (both sides are two disjoint 4-cycles plus two isolated vertices); minimality by the exact census EXP-C14-04
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  2K1[C4 + K1] and (2K2 + K1)[2K1] are both isomorphic to 2C4 + 2K1; C4 + K1 and 2K2 + K1 are prime (order 5),
  so 2C4 + 2K1 has two lexicographic factorisations with different multisets of prime factors. No such
  collision occurs below order 10.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C14.md#theorem-11-clm-c14-11--a-lexicographic-collision-at-order-10
- **evidence:** EXP-C14-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  Non-uniqueness of lexicographic prime factorisation (with an invariant number of factors) is due to Imrich; cited via arXiv:2407.02615 and the Handbook of Product Graphs. The specific minimal example was not looked up.

- **references:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02615

### CLM-C14-12: Code 141 (EA AE AA NN) has unique factorisation on connected graphs (equivalently, symmetric difference on co-connected graphs)

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  Every connected graph has exactly one factorisation into connected primes with respect to the product
  (g,h) ~ (g',h') iff (EA, AE, AA or NN); equivalently every graph with connected complement factors uniquely
  under the symmetric-difference product into primes with connected complement.

- **evidence:** EXP-C14-04
- **limitations:**

  Exhaustive only for orders <= 15 inside the submonoid of connected graphs (factors with <= 7 vertices); the mechanism that breaks uniqueness for connected graphs under xor (absorption of switching by K2, Theorem 7) is unavailable because 2K1 is disconnected, which explains but does not prove the observation. The Skeptic's own census finds no collision for 141 on connected graphs at any order <= 15.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C14-13: H-C14-4: two-graphs form a free commutative monoid under the symmetric-difference (Kronecker) product

- **cycle:** C14
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  Every switching class of finite graphs has a unique factorisation into prime switching classes with respect
  to the product induced by the symmetric difference (equivalently, symmetric +-1 matrices with unit diagonal
  up to signed permutation similarity, under the Kronecker product).

- **evidence:** EXP-C14-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence. The product of two-graphs is the Kronecker product of the matrices S + I, as used for graphical Hadamard matrices (Abiad-Butler-Haemers, arXiv:1801.01149 sections 4-5). Cancellation by [K_2] is Oh-Yoo-Yun (SIAM J. Discrete Math. 2013). No unique-factorisation statement for two-graphs, or for +-1 matrices up to signed permutation similarity, was found. Lajou's unique factorisation of signed graphs concerns the Cartesian product (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **limitations:**

  No collision among all factorisations of orders <= 16 (orders 4, 6, 8, 9, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16); class counts validated against A002854 for n <= 8. Prime two-graph counts (exact for n <= 16): 1, 2, 2, 7, 14, 54, 240, 2035, 33113, ... Cancellation by [K2] is proved (CLM-C14-07c). The Skeptic notes that this evidence was thin - up to order 16 almost every composite is [K2] (x) X, covered by the proved cancellation - and extends it substantially: all 53,703 multisets of prime classes with <= 8 vertices and product order <= 64, screened by exact integer power sums tr((S+I)^k) and decided by exact switching-equivalence tests (2,205 cospectral groups, 3,012 pairs), give 0 collisions.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C15-01: Light cones of semigroups - the popcount word, Pascal exponents, bounded streaming width

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib for the commutative formula (ca_comm_binomial, ca_step_shift; standard axioms only); written proof for the word form and the width bound (proofs/paper/C15.md Theorem 1); every order-3 semigroup has width <= 3 (EXP-C15-02); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For an associative magma the t-step local map is the ordered product F_t(x_0..x_t) = prod_{j < 2^t} x_{s(j)},
  s(j) the binary digit sum of j; for a commutative semigroup F_t = prod_k x_k^C(t,k), and the forward and
  backward streaming widths of F_t are at most |S|.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C15.md#theorem-1-clm-c15-01--light-cones-of-semigroups
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C15_MagmaCA.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-02; EXP-C15-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Pascal's triangle for additive CA is classical (Martin, Odlyzko, Wolfram, Comm. Math. Phys. 93 (1984)); automata of the form c'_i = c_i * c_{i+1} over groupoids, groups and quasigroups were studied by J. Pedersen (Complex Systems 6 (1992) 237-250) and C. Moore (Moore-Drisko 1995/96; Moore-Pnin, arXiv:patt-sol/9701008), where the word structure of semigroup and group rules is used. The width bound is immediate.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C15-02: Affine magmas - binomial light cone and an O(L log L log t) fast-forward

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C15.md Theorem 2); implementation agrees with direct simulation in all 55 affine cases (EXP-C15-03); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For x*y = phi(x) + psi(y) + c on an abelian group A with commuting endomorphisms phi, psi:
  F_t = sum_k C(t,k) phi^(t-k) psi^k (x_k) + sum_(s<t) (phi + psi)^s (c); on cyclic rows the row at time t is
  P^t r + (sum_(s<t) q^s) c with P = phi + psi S in End(A)[S]/(S^L - 1), computable by binary exponentiation
  (for A = Z_n with FFT products, O(L log L log t)); streaming width <= |A|.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C15.md#theorem-2-clm-c15-02--affine-magmas-and-their-fast-forward
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Linear and affine cellular automata (O. Martin, A. M. Odlyzko, S. Wolfram, Algebraic properties of cellular automata, Comm. Math. Phys. 93 (1984) 219-258; M. Ito, N. Osato, M. Nasu 1983); the operator-power form is standard.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C15-03: Commutative semigroups - fast-forward by exponent arithmetic

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C15.md Theorem 3); implementation agrees with direct simulation for three commutative semigroups (EXP-C15-03); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md) (it notes that t >= max(L + 1, iota) already suffices)

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  For a finite commutative semigroup and t >= 2L + iota + 1 (iota the largest index of an element), cell i of a cyclic row at time t is prod_j r_(i+j)^(N_j), where N_j are the coefficients of (1 + S)^t mod S^L - 1, and only N_j modulo the lcm of the element periods is needed.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C15.md#theorem-3-clm-c15-03--commutative-semigroups-exponent-arithmetic
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-03
- **novelty_basis:** A direct combination of Theorem 1(b) with the index/period structure of cyclic semigroups; likely folklore.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C15-04: The fuse magma: linear streaming width, yet an O(L) fast-forward for every t

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C15.md Theorem 4); fast-forward agrees with direct simulation on 27,000 (row, t) pairs during development and in all EXP-C15-03 cases; widths and the lower-bound residual family checked to t = 12 (EXP-C15-05); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md) (the upper bound needs one more sentence, given in the review A.2; burn-time formula checked on 870,969 exhaustive cases; lambda(t) = t + 2 for t <= 14)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For the fuse magma [[0,0,0],[0,0,1],[1,2,2]] (0 burnt, 1 burning, 2 intact): burn times satisfy tau_i = 0 (x_i = 0),
  tau_(i+1) + 2 (x_i = 2), tau_(i+1) (x_i = 1, x_(i+1) = 2), 1 (x_i = 1, x_(i+1) != 2); the row at any time t is
  obtained from them in O(L) operations; and floor((m+1)/2) <= lambda(t) <= 3t + 3 with m = floor(t/2), so the
  streaming width grows linearly (computed lambda(t) = t + 2 for t <= 12).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C15.md#theorem-4-clm-c15-04--the-fuse-magma-linear-streaming-width-constant-time-fast-forward
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-02; EXP-C15-03; EXP-C15-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence. Streaming width is essentially the one-way communication complexity of the t-step local rule used by Durr-Rapaport-Theyssier (TCS 322 (2004) 355-368, https://arxiv.org/abs/cs/0210008; their d_n uses the central cut and the maximum over the two directions). They classify ECAs into bounded/linear/other and prove linear complexity with an explicit description of f^n for rule 132 (Lemma 10), so 'unbounded width yet easy prediction' is already exemplified there. The fuse magma and its burn-time fast-forward were not found (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **references:** https://arxiv.org/abs/cs/0210008
- **status history:**

### CLM-C15-05: Surjective iff permutive for magmas of order at most 3; false at order 4

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  computer-assisted - exact pair-graph (Garden-of-Eden) decision for all 16 tables of order 2 and all 1734 classes of order 3 (invariant under isomorphism and mirror image), Hedlund's theorem for permutive => surjective; the order-4 example by a two-line argument (identity x shift) and the same decision procedure (EXP-C15-05)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For |M| <= 3 the automaton c'_i = c_i * c_{i+1} is surjective on bi-infinite rows iff all rows or all columns of the table are permutations (1, 6, 420 surjective tables of orders 1, 2, 3 = 2(n!)^n - Lat(n)); for |M| = 4 the rectangular band (b,c)*(b',c') = (b,c') is surjective and injective but not permutive.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C15.md#theorem-5-clm-c15-05--surjective-iff-permutive-for-m--3
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-01; EXP-C15-02; EXP-C15-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  J. Kari, V. Salo, I. Torma, Surjective two-neighbor cellular automata on prime alphabets (2013) - surjective two-neighbour CA over prime alphabets are permutive in some coordinate (https://research.utu.fi/converis/portal/detail/Publication/3119276; notes https://villesalo.com/notes/STNCAoPANotes.html); G. A. Hedlund, Math. Systems Theory 3 (1969). Our exhaustive check is an independent confirmation for n = 2, 3; the order-4 band illustrates why primality is needed.


### CLM-C15-06: The two-element magma automata are the elementary rules 17m

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written one-line proof (proofs/paper/C15.md Theorem 6) and exhaustive computation (EXP-C15-01)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:** The 16 automata of two-element magmas are exactly the elementary cellular automata whose rule ignores the left neighbour, the Wolfram numbers 0, 17, 34, ..., 255.
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C15.md#theorem-6-clm-c15-06--order-2
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-01
- **novelty_basis:** Elementary-rule numbering (S. Wolfram, Rev. Mod. Phys. 55 (1983)); rules independent of one neighbour are a standard subfamily.

### CLM-C15-07: Census of order-3 magma automata by streaming width

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  Of the 1734 order-3 magma automata (up to isomorphism and mirror image), on t <= 9 the streaming width is bounded for 808 classes, grows linearly for 104, geometrically for 300 (largest lambda(9) = 649) and irregularly for 522; 43 are surjective, 7 injective and 34 nilpotent by t = 9.

- **evidence:** EXP-C15-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence. A census in the style of Durr-Rapaport-Theyssier's experimental classification of the 88 elementary CA (TCS 322 (2004), Table 1); the magma-automaton framework is Pedersen's (1992) and Moore's. No census of the 1734 order-3 magma automata by this measure was found (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **limitations:** Growth classes are judged on t <= 9 only and may change for larger t; 'linear' and 'geometric' are empirical labels except for the fuse magma (Theorem 4).
- **status history:**

### CLM-C15-08: H-C15-1: streaming width is either bounded or geometric (refuted)

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For every magma automaton of order <= 3 the streaming width lambda(t) is either bounded or grows geometrically; there is no intermediate growth.
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-02; EXP-C15-05
- **counterexample:** The fuse magma [[0,0,0],[0,0,1],[1,2,2]] has floor((floor(t/2)+1)/2) <= lambda(t) <= 3t + 3 (Theorem 4; lambda(t) = t + 2 for t <= 12); 104 order-3 classes show linear growth on t <= 9 (EXP-C15-02).

### CLM-C15-09: H-C15-2: bounded width exactly for semigroups, affine magmas and bounded light cones (refuted)

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** An order-3 magma automaton has bounded streaming width iff the magma is a semigroup, is affine over Z_3, or its light cone depends on at most two cells.
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-02
- **counterexample:**

  The 'if' direction holds for all 18 semigroup and 8 affine classes, but 685 bounded-width classes lie outside the three classes, e.g. the reversible automaton [[0,0,2],[2,2,0],[1,1,1]] whose light cone uses all t + 1 cells and whose width stays 3 (EXP-C15-02).


### CLM-C15-10: H-C15-4: an automaton can be fast-forwarded iff its light-cone complexity is bounded (refuted)

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** A magma automaton admits a fast-forward (rows at time t computable in time polynomial in L and log t) if and only if its streaming width is bounded.
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-03; EXP-C15-05
- **counterexample:** The fuse magma has unbounded (linear) streaming width but its row at any time t is computable in O(L) operations (Theorem 4). The converse direction is open (CLM-C15-18).

### CLM-C15-11: H-C15-5: cycle detection fast-forwards most automata, and long cycles mean geometric width (refuted in part)

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For at least half of the order-3 classes a random cyclic row of length 32 repeats within 5000 steps, and only classes with geometric streaming width have rows that do not.
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-04
- **counterexample:**

  The first part holds (1590/1734 classes at L = 32), the second fails - the bounded-width affine quasigroups x + y, 2x + y, 2x + 2y, 2x + 2y + 1 (mod 3, up to relabelling) do not repeat within 5000 steps at L = 32 and 64 (EXP-C15-04). The cap matters (Skeptic) - at L = 32 they do repeat, with periods 6,560 or 19,680 (still long, so the refutation stands); at L = 64 none repeats within 200,000 steps.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C15-12: H-C15-6: every exact fast-forward beats simulation by more than 100x at L = 1024, t = 10^5 and loses for small t (refuted)

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** Each of the affine, commutative-semigroup and fuse fast-forwards is exact, beats direct numpy simulation by more than 100x at L = 1024, t = 10^5, and loses for t below a few hundred steps.
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-03
- **counterexample:**

  Exactness holds (99/99), but the commutative-semigroup method reaches only x62 (mult mod 3) and x77 (min) at L = 1024, t = 10^5 (the Skeptic's re-run - 79-97x). The clause 'loses for small t' is partly true - timed with the same protocol on both sides, the affine method loses for t below about 20 and the fuse method below about 20-70 (Skeptic review, Appendix C). The first version said the affine and fuse methods never lose; EXP-C15-03 had timed the fast method as the best of 3 runs and the simulation as a single run.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C15-13: Affine fast-forward by operator powers

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** algorithmic
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **improvement:** IMPROVEMENT
- **statement:**

  Computing the row at time t of an affine magma automaton by binary exponentiation of P = a + bS modulo S^L - 1 (FFT products) is exact and, at L = 1024, faster than direct numpy simulation by about one order of magnitude at t = 10^3, two orders at 10^4 (138-314x in two runs) and three orders at 10^5 (1,459-2,500x); t = 10^9 takes milliseconds for L <= 4096. With the same timing protocol on both sides it loses for t below about 20.

- **evidence:** EXP-C15-03
- **novelty_basis:** Standard for linear CA (Martin-Odlyzko-Wolfram 1984); the benchmark is ours.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C15-14: Commutative-semigroup fast-forward by exponent arithmetic

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** algorithmic
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **improvement:** MIXED

  The exponent-arithmetic fast-forward (Theorem 3) is exact; at L = 1024 it is about two orders of magnitude faster than direct simulation at t = 10^5 (62-100x; the Skeptic's re-run 79-97x) but no faster around t = 10^3 (0.5-1.0x) for L >= 1024, and equal for t < 2L (where it falls back to simulation).

### CLM-C15-15: Fuse fast-forward by burn times

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** algorithmic
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **improvement:** IMPROVEMENT
- **statement:**

  The burn-time fast-forward of the fuse magma (Theorem 4) is exact and takes O(L) time for every t - about three orders of magnitude faster than direct simulation at t = 10^5 (L = 1024; 4,021x in the first run, 1,363x on the Skeptic's re-run), with t = 10^9 in about a millisecond; because the O(L) method is a Python loop it loses to simulation for t below about 20-70.

- **cycle:** C15

- **improvement:** MIXED

  Storing visited rows answers a query t in mu + lambda simulated steps; for random rows of length 32 this is at most 5000 steps for 1590 of 1734 order-3 classes (median transient 5, median period 2), but it fails for the affine quasigroups and for 128 of 300 geometric-width classes.

### CLM-C15-17: Earlier structures of the lab as automata

- **cycle:** C15

  The Laver tables A_2 and A_3 give automata whose light cones depend on at most 3 resp. 4 cells (t <= 8 resp. 5) with streaming (forward) widths 4 and 8 (backward widths 5 and 13-14); GF(4) multiplication and XOR on [0,4) have streaming width 4; the anchored golden- and odd-law models of C06 are permutive (hence surjective) affine automata.

### CLM-C15-18: Bounded streaming width implies a fast-forward

- **cycle:** C15
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** CONJECTURE
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** Every magma automaton with bounded streaming width admits an algorithm computing a cyclic row of length L at time t in time polynomial in L and log t.
- **evidence:** EXP-C15-02; EXP-C15-03
- **limitations:**

  True for affine magmas and commutative semigroups (Theorems 2-3); no algorithm is known for the 685 bounded-width order-3 classes outside the structured classes, and bounded width does not give short cycles (the affine quasigroups have long ones).


### CLM-C16-01: The Fibonacci pair map of a magma is a permutation iff every left translation is a bijection

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (pairMap_injective_iff, pairMap_bijective_iff, mirrorMap_eq; standard axioms only); count by written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 1) and exhaustive check n <= 4 (EXP-C16-01)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For a finite magma (M, *), the pair map P(a, b) = (b, b*a) of the recurrence x_{k+1} = x_k * x_{k-1} is a
  bijection iff every left translation a -> b*a is a bijection; hence exactly (n!)^n of the n^(n^2) tables of
  order n give permutations. The mirror recurrence x_{k+1} = x_{k-1} * x_k is the forward recurrence of the
  opposite magma.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-1-clm-c16-01--when-the-pair-map-is-a-permutation
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C16_Dynamics.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Every function M x M -> M is the feedback of a unique magma, so pair maps are exactly the order-2 feedback shift registers over n letters, and the criterion is the q-ary form of Golomb's nonsingularity condition (S. W. Golomb, Shift Register Sequences, 1967; see e.g. arXiv:1911.06670 for the binary statement and the correspondence of nonsingular registers with factors of the de Bruijn graph).


### CLM-C16-02: H-C16-1: maximal period at most n^2 - 1, attained only by quasigroups (refuted)

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** As for Pisano periods (where (0, 0) is fixed), the maximal period of the pair map on an n-element magma is at most n^2 - 1, and the maximum is attained only by quasigroups.
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-01
- **counterexample:**

  x*y = 1 - y on {0,1} has the single cycle (0,0) -> (0,1) -> (1,1) -> (1,0) of length 4 = n^2 and is not a quasigroup; every n has full-period tables (Theorem 2); for n <= 4 none of them is a quasigroup (EXP-C16-01).


### CLM-C16-03: Full-period magmas are de Bruijn cycles: there are (n!)^n / n^2 of them

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 2) using the van Aardenne-Ehrenfest--de Bruijn count; exhaustive confirmation for n = 2, 3, 4 (EXP-C16-01); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  A table of order n is full-period (its pair map is one cycle through all n^2 states) iff the symbol sequence
  along that cycle is a de Bruijn cycle of order 2 over n symbols, and this is a bijection; hence there are exactly
  (n!)^n / n^2 full-period tables of order n (1, 24, 20736, ...) and the maximal period over n-element magmas is
  n^2 for every n >= 1.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-2-clm-c16-03--full-period-is-the-same-thing-as-a-de-bruijn-cycle
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Translation of a classical fact - the number of de Bruijn sequences B(k, m) is (k!)^(k^(m-1)) / k^m (T. van Aardenne-Ehrenfest, N. G. de Bruijn, Simon Stevin 28 (1951); BEST theorem; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Bruijn_sequence).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-04: A parity condition for full periods

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 3, factorisation P = swap o fibrewise translations); satisfied by every full-period table found (EXP-C16-01/02); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** If every left translation L_b is a bijection, sign(P) = (-1)^(n(n-1)/2) prod_b sgn(L_b); hence a full-period table satisfies prod_b sgn(L_b) = (-1)^((n-1)(n+2)/2).
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-3-clm-c16-04--a-parity-condition
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-02
- **novelty_basis:** Elementary; likely known in the shift-register literature in other forms (Golomb's cycle-parity results for binary registers); no search for the q-ary order-2 statement beyond two web searches.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-05: Full-period quasigroups are cycles in which every pair occurs once at distance 1 and once at distance 2

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 4); the exhaustive Latin-square count and the independent distance-1/2 DFS count agree (EXP-C16-02); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  A full-period table is a quasigroup iff in its cycle x_0 ... x_{n^2-1} every ordered pair also occurs exactly once
  as (x_i, x_{i+2}); equivalently the windows (x_{i-1}, x_i, x_{i+1}) run once through the n^2 triples
  (a, b, b*a) of the Latin square (a universal cycle of the quasigroup).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-4-clm-c16-05--full-period-quasigroups-are-distance-2-de-bruijn-cycles
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Full-period tables are Klimov's finite UFS-groupoids with periodic universal Fibonacci sequence (P. Klimov, 'Universal Fibonacci sequences and UFS-groupoids', arXiv:2604.05754, 2026, Thm 4.2: their cycles are the de Bruijn sequences B(n,2)). Klimov characterises them through distance-1 pairs only and leaves open whether one can be a quasigroup (Problem 7.5). The distance-2 characterisation was not found. Tuscan-2 squares (Etzion-Golomb-Taylor 1989) and recursive MDS codes are different objects. Small (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **references:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05754
- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-06: H-C16-5: no quasigroup has a full-period pair map (refuted)

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For every n, no quasigroup of order n has a full-period pair map; the maximal period of a quasigroup of order n is n^2 - 1.
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-02
- **counterexample:**

  True for n = 2, 3, 4, false for n = 5 - exactly 3360 of the 161280 Latin squares of order 5 are full-period (e.g. the cycle 0,0,1,0,2,2,0,3,3,1,2,1,4,0,4,4,2,3,2,4,1,1,3,4,3); examples exist for every order 6-16 (EXP-C16-02).


### CLM-C16-07: Full-period quasigroups: none of order 2, 3, 4; 3360 of order 5; examples of every order 6-16

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  computer-assisted - exhaustive analysis of all Latin squares of orders 2-5, confirmed by an independent depth-first enumeration (0, 0, 0, 3360); explicit certificates for orders 6-16 re-verified by an independent cycle analysis; products by Theorem 6 (EXP-C16-02)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  There is no full-period quasigroup of order 2, 3 or 4; there are exactly 3360 of order 5, forming 28 isomorphism
  classes; full-period quasigroups of every order 6, ..., 16 and of orders 30, 35, 40, 42, 45, 56, 63, 72 exist.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-5-clm-c16-07--which-orders-have-full-period-quasigroups-computer-assisted
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Answers Problems 7.3-7.5 of P. Klimov (arXiv:2604.05754v2, 2026) positively, with finite examples: he asks whether a UFS-groupoid (finite or infinite) with more than one element can be left-cancellative, a left quasigroup or a quasigroup; the lab's order-5 cycle gives a Latin square whose pair map is one 25-cycle (checked independently by the Historian, reviews/historian_checks/C11_C20/klimov_problem75_check.py). The counts 0, 0, 0, 3360 were not found. Small (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **references:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05754
- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-08: Direct products of full-period magmas of coprime orders are full-period

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 6, Chinese remainder theorem on cycle lengths n1^2, n2^2); eight products verified (EXP-C16-02); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** If two magmas (or quasigroups) of coprime orders n1, n2 are full-period, so is their direct product (a quasigroup if both are).
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-6-clm-c16-08--products-of-coprime-orders

### CLM-C16-09: No isotope of a group of even order has a full-period Fibonacci pair map

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 7, parity of the regular representation via Burnside's normal 2-complement theorem); consistent with 18000 random isotopes of the nine groups of order 4, 6, 8 (EXP-C16-02); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md) (the review checked the Burnside step and 36 groups of even order 2-30; it also filled the evidence gap for n = 5 - 480 of the 3360 full-period quasigroups of order 5 are isotopes of Z_5)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For every group G of even order n = 2m and every isotope x o y = gamma(alpha(x) beta(y)), prod_b sgn(L_b) = (-1)^m, whereas a full period requires (-1)^(m+1); so no group of even order and no isotope of one is full-period. (For odd n isotopes of Z_n are full-period for every n = 5, ..., 15.)

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-7-clm-c16-09--no-isotope-of-a-group-of-even-order-is-full-period
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  The sign of the left regular representation is classical; its use as an obstruction for full Fibonacci periods was not found (searches as for CLM-C16-05; Fibonacci lengths of groups - Knox, Fibonacci Quart. 30 (1992) 116-120; Campbell-Campbell, Irish Math. Soc. Bull. 56 (2005) - study generating pairs, not full periods). Small. Sign-of-permutation obstructions to single cycles of shift-register maps are standard (e.g. S. Li, arXiv:2605.09489, 2026). Klimov's study of UFS-groupoids (arXiv:2604.05754) contains no parity or isotopy result. The q-ary NFSR literature was not searched exhaustively for an even-alphabet obstruction (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-10: Full-period quasigroups exist for every order n >= 5

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For every n >= 5 there is a quasigroup of order n whose Fibonacci pair map is a single cycle through all n^2 pairs.
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-02; EXP-C16-05
- **limitations:**

  Verified for n = 5..16 and for the products 30, 35, 40, 42, 45, 56, 63, 72; the Skeptic's own sampler adds verified examples for every n = 17..26 and full-period Z_n-isotopes for odd n <= 21. Sampling hit rates are of the order 1/n^2 per random Latin square but not close to it (exact n = 5 rate 0.52/n^2; pooled over n = 6..16 the lab has 139 hits against 181 expected), so a count of order L(n)/n^2 (L(n) = number of Latin squares) is suggested, without proof. Lexicographic DFS (3e6 nodes) and SAT (3e5 conflicts) fail from n = 9 on, random sampling succeeds (EXP-C16-05).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-11: Fixed points of the pair map are idempotents

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (pairMap_fixed_iff; standard axioms only)
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** P(a, b) = (a, b) iff a = b and a*a = a; an idempotent magma has n fixed points and maximal period at most n^2 - n, so no idempotent quasigroup is full-period.
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-8-clm-c16-11--fixed-points-are-idempotents
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C16_Dynamics.lean

### CLM-C16-12: Affine magmas: periods are orders of companion matrices; the anchored magma x + y + 1 is the Fibonacci sequence mod N

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 9, conjugation to the linear map and a cyclic vector); Lean for the anchored conjugacy (anchored_fibonacci); exhaustive confirmation for anchored N <= 60, odd N <= 61 and 51 golden pairs (EXP-C16-03); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For x*y = alpha x + beta y + gamma on Z/N (beta a unit) whose pair map has a fixed point, the maximal period is the
  order of [[0,1],[beta,alpha]] mod N. In particular the anchored associative magma x + y + 1 has exactly the cycle
  structure of the Fibonacci numbers mod N (maximal period = the Pisano period), the odd-law magma x + 1 + y/2 has
  maximal period ord([[0,1],[1/2,1]]) and the golden-law magmas x + 1 + a y (a^2 + a = 1) have maximal period
  ord([[0,1],[a,1]]).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-9-clm-c16-12--affine-magmas
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C16_Dynamics.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Periods of second-order linear recurrences modulo N are orders of companion matrices (M. Ward, Trans. AMS 35 (1933); D. D. Wall, Amer. Math. Monthly 67 (1960) for Fibonacci). The resulting period sequences of the odd and golden laws (6, 24, 48, 18, 5, ... and 4, 120, 342, ...) are not in the OEIS.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-13: For affine magmas the maximal period equals the order of the 3x3 affine matrix

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For every affine magma x*y = alpha x + beta y + gamma on Z/N with beta a unit, the maximal period of the pair map equals the order of [[0,1,0],[beta,alpha,gamma],[0,0,1]] mod N.
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-03
- **limitations:**

  300 of 300 random cases with N <= 30 in the lab; the Skeptic checked all 1,997,716 affine magmas with beta a unit and 2 <= N <= 60 exhaustively - the maximal cycle length equals the lcm of all cycle lengths in every case, and that lcm equals the directly computed matrix order for N <= 24. Proven only when the pair map has a fixed point (CLM-C16-12); a partial proof idea from the review - if gamma is a unit, e_3 is a cyclic vector (det[e_3, A e_3, A^2 e_3] = -gamma^2).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-14: Fibonacci sequences of nimbers: maximal period pi(2^(2^k) - 1)

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 10, discrete logarithms in the field of nimbers); confirmed for q = 16, 256 (EXP-C16-03); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  On [0, q), q = 2^(2^k), with nim-multiplication the pair map sends pairs containing 0 to (0, 0) within two steps and is isomorphic on nonzero pairs to the Fibonacci pair map on (Z/(q-1))^2; its maximal period is pi(q - 1) (40 for q = 16, 360 for q = 256).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-10-clm-c16-14--fibonacci-sequences-of-nimbers
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-03
- **novelty_basis:** The Fibonacci length of a cyclic group is its Pisano period (classical; Knox 1992, Campbell et al.); nimber fields are Conway's.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-15: Laver tables: every Fibonacci orbit ends in the fixed point or one of the N - 1 two-cycles through the top element

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 11) from Laver's facts (N-1)*y = y, x*(N-1) = N-1, x*y > x for x < N-1 (Dehornoy, Braids and Self-Distributivity, Ch. X), which EXP-C16-03 also checks for n <= 10; Lean for the 2-cycle lemma (top_two_cycle); cycle structure confirmed for n <= 10; skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C14_C15_C16_C18.md)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For n >= 1 and N = 2^n, the pair map of the Laver table A_n (0-based) has exactly N cycles - the fixed point
  (N-1, N-1) and the two-cycles {(N-1, c), (c, N-1)} - and every orbit reaches one of them; so the maximal period is 2
  (H-C16-4, 'the maximal period is a power of 2', holds for this degenerate reason).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-11-clm-c16-15--laver-tables-collapse-to-2-cycles
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C16_Dynamics.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence; trivial corollary. No source on Fibonacci-type recurrences in Laver tables was found (Biane, SLC 80 (2019) B80c; Dehornoy's surveys; Klimov's UFS paper, arXiv:2604.05754, does not mention Laver tables). It is a two-line consequence of p * q > p (p < 2^n) and p * 2^n = 2^n (Dehornoy-Lebed, arXiv:1401.2335, (1.3)-(1.4)). Not a substantive novelty (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-16: Fibonacci orbits in Laver tables reach the top element within 5 steps (n <= 10)

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** The longest transient of the Laver pair map (steps until the state contains the top element) is 1, 3, 3 for n = 1, 2, 3 and exactly 5 for every n = 4, ..., 10 (the Skeptic - every n = 4, ..., 13).
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-03
- **limitations:** No explanation; the proof of CLM-C16-15 only gives the bound N.

### CLM-C16-17: Magma Fibonacci generators are not competitive pseudo-random generators

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** algorithmic
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **improvement:** NO IMPROVEMENT
- **statement:**

  On a simple battery (byte chi-square, serial pairs, lowest-bit period, lag-1 correlation; 2^20 outputs) the anchored Fibonacci and affine magma generators on Z/2^32 fail low-byte uniformity and have lowest-bit period 3, and the de Bruijn byte magma (maximal possible period 65536) is too regular; PCG64 passes every test.

- **evidence:** EXP-C16-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low-bit periodicity of additive generators modulo 2^w and the non-uniformity of Fibonacci numbers modulo 2^k are classical (Knuth, TAOCP vol. 2, 3.2.2; H. Niederreiter, Fibonacci Quart. 10 (1972) - uniform distribution only modulo powers of 5).

- **limitations:** A sanity battery, not TestU01 or PractRand. The 'too regular' verdict on the de Bruijn generator is automatic - 2^20 outputs are exactly 16 full periods (Skeptic, P17).
- **status history:**

### CLM-C16-18: Maximal Fibonacci periods of small groups

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** computer-assisted - exhaustive functional-graph analysis of the pair maps (EXP-C16-03)
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** Over all pairs of elements, the maximal period of x_{k+1} = x_k x_{k-1} is 8 in S3, 6 in D4, 6 in Q8, 16 in A4 and 18 in S4.

### CLM-C16-19: Random sampling finds full-period quasigroups where exact search fails

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  With fixed budgets, lexicographic DFS (3e6 nodes) and a SAT encoding (3e5 conflicts) find full-period quasigroups only up to n = 8, while seeded sampling of random Latin squares finds them for every n = 5..16 at rates close to 1/n^2 (H-C16-11).

### CLM-C17-01: Light's test: generators suffice for associativity

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C17.md Theorem 1, four-line closure argument); 0 wrong verdicts in EXP-C17-01, skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C07_C17_C20.md) (the Skeptic re-checked every generating set of every magma of order <= 3 and 236,381 generating sets of 20,000 random order-4 tables - 0 wrong verdicts)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:** If S generates the finite magma M, then M is associative iff (x*s)*y = x*(s*y) for all x, y in M and s in S; the test costs 2n + 2n^2 probes per generator.
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C17.md#theorem-1-clm-c17-01--lights-associativity-test
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-01
- **novelty_basis:** F. W. Light's associativity test, presented in A. H. Clifford, G. B. Preston, The Algebraic Theory of Semigroups, Vol. 1 (AMS, 1961), section 1.2.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C17-02: Associativity of an anchored magma is decided by one O(n^2) identity

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C17.md Corollary 2) from C06 Theorem 1 (Lean anchored_assoc_unique); 0 wrong verdicts on 30 anchored instances (EXP-C17-01)
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** If x*0 = x + 1 (mod n), then * is associative iff x*(y + 1) = (x*y) + 1 for all x, y (2n^2 probes); equivalently Light's test with the single generator 0.
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C17.md#corollary-2-clm-c17-02--anchored-magmas-one-generator-and-an-on-criterion
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-01; EXP-C17-02
- **novelty_basis:** Immediate from Light's test and the lab's C06 Theorem 1; small.

### CLM-C17-03: The Rajagopalan-Schulman randomised associativity test is one-sided

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  one-sidedness proved (proofs/paper/C17.md Theorem 3, bilinear extension); the 1/8 detection bound is the cited theorem of Rajagopalan-Schulman, not re-proved; empirically 0 errors in 90 instances with 40 rounds

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The GF(2) subset-sum test (compare (u*v)*w and u*(v*w) in the magma algebra for random subsets u, v, w) never rejects an associative table, and rejects a non-associative one with probability at least 1/8 per round; each round costs O(n^2) probes.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C17.md#theorem-3-clm-c17-03--the-rajagopalanschulman-test-is-one-sided
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-01
- **novelty_basis:** S. Rajagopalan, L. J. Schulman, Verification of identities, SIAM J. Comput. 29 (2000) 1155-1168.

### CLM-C17-04: Benchmark - generator and anchor tests versus the exhaustive check

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** algorithmic
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **improvement:** MIXED
- **statement:**

  Certification (associative inputs, n = 32..512) - Light's test with a greedy generating set uses 23-383 times (cyclic groups) and 38-614 times (anchored tables) fewer table probes than the exhaustive n^3 check (generating-set cost included), the anchored test 2n^2 probes; on left-zero bands the gain is only 1.7x. Refutation (one-entry corruptions, random magmas) - an exhaustive check that stops at the first violating triple is about as cheap as Light's test or much cheaper (4 probes on random magmas against about 9e5; 0.1-1.1x Light's probes on corruptions), so the gains concern certification only. The randomised test (40 rounds) made no error; random-triple sampling with n^2/16 samples missed 27 of 30 near-associative inputs.

- **evidence:** EXP-C17-01
- **novelty_basis:** The algorithms are classical (Light; Rajagopalan-Schulman); the benchmark is ours. Timings were measured on a shared, loaded machine and are indicative only; probe counts are exact.
- **limitations:**

  Families chosen to include worst cases for each method; no comparison with optimised C implementations. The first version reported 23-77x gains on corrupted and random inputs too; the Skeptic showed those ratios measured an accounting artefact (the exhaustive check was charged a whole 64-row slab as soon as the slab contained a violation). Fixed in systems/law_testing.naive and re-measured.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C17-05: The anchor instance of left-distributivity singles out the Laver tables (every N <= 4096)

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  computer-assisted - for every N, uniqueness and existence of the table forced by the instances y <= N - 2, and the fact that it satisfies the full instance iff the wrap-around equations hold, are Lean-checked (proofs/lean/Infinity/C17_ForcedTable.lean: row_last, row_gt, forced_unique, forced_existsUnique, full_instance_iff; standard axioms); the wrap-around equations were evaluated on the forced table for every N <= 4096 (EXP-C17-03, recorded run; the Skeptic's independent C program agrees); at N = 2^n the forced table is A_n because A_n is left-distributive (Laver's theorem, published) and so satisfies the instances. The written uniqueness argument is the Skeptic's (reviews/skeptic_C07_C17_C20.md A.5); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-19 (reviews/skeptic_batch1.1.md, the Lean statements are faithful)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For every N, the instances y <= N - 2 of x*(y+1) = (x*y)*(x+1) force at most one anchored table (Laver's recursion; written proof in proofs/paper/C17.md). At N = 2^n it is the Laver table A_n, which satisfies the full instance including the wrap-around y = N - 1. For every N <= 4096 (EXP-C17-03, and independently the Skeptic's C program) the full instance has a model exactly at the powers of 2, so for these N left-distributivity of an anchored table is decided by an O(N^2) check.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C17.md#observation-4-clm-c17-05--the-anchor-instance-of-left-distributivity
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C17_ForcedTable.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-03; EXP-C17-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Uniqueness for every N: Dehornoy, Braids and Self-Distributivity (2000), Lemma X.1.2; Biane, Sem. Lothar. Combin. 80 (2019) B80c section 2.1 (https://www.mat.univie.ac.at/~slc/wpapers/s80biane.pdf). Non-existence of a model of the full instance for N not a power of 2: Biane's footnote 1 ('(2.2) for all p, q ... if N is not a power of 2 this is not true', credited to A. Lewandowski); Aguilera-Iannella (arXiv:2610.06106, 2026) state it as Biane's observation, and it follows from their Prop. 2.5 (the instance at (a, N) holds iff a * N = N) together with Dehornoy's Lemma X.1.7 and Prop. X.1.6. So the statement holds for every N (CLM-C17-08) (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **references:** https://www.mat.univie.ac.at/~slc/wpapers/s80biane.pdf; https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06106
- **limitations:** Uniqueness and existence are Lean-checked for every N; that non-powers of 2 have no model is checked here for N <= 4096 and proved for every N in CLM-C17-08 (Lean, from Laver's theorem).
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-19T23:04:05Z verification: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED → PROVEN (sync:C17; Batch 1.1 - uniqueness, existence and the reduction to the wrap-around equations Lean-checked (C17_ForcedTable.lean), so the bounded statement is a computer-assisted proof: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED …)
  - 2026-10-19T23:04:05Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C17; Batch 1.1 - uniqueness, existence and the reduction to the wrap-around equations Lean-checked (C17_ForcedTable.lean), so the bounded statement is a computer-assisted proof: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED …)

### CLM-C17-06: H-C17-1: Light's test beats the exhaustive check by a factor >= n/10 on every family (refuted)

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** Light's test with a greedy generating set uses at most a fraction 10/n of the exhaustive check's probes on every input family.
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-01
- **counterexample:**

  Left-zero band x*y = x (associative; n generators needed) - exhaustive/Light probe ratio 1.71 at n = 512 (EXP-C17-01). The first version also cited one-entry corruptions of cyclic groups (ratio 47.9 at n = 512); that number was an artefact of the probe accounting (Skeptic review, P1). With correct accounting an early-exit exhaustive check is about as cheap as Light's test on corruptions and far cheaper on random magmas, so the hypothesis fails on non-associative inputs as well.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C17-07: H-C17-2: for anchored magmas the z = 0 instance of a law decides the law (refuted)

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For every law of the C06 family, every anchored magma satisfying the instance z = 0 of the law satisfies the law (N <= 5).
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-02
- **counterexample:**

  43 of the 382 laws (first violating anchored model at N = 3 for 33 laws, at N = 4 for 10), e.g. x(yz) = x(zy) (54 violating models among 82 at N = 3), x(yz) = y(y(xz)) (two violating models among 11 at N = 4), x(yz) = x((xy)z), (xy)z = (zy)x, (xy)z = (x(zz))y (EXP-C17-02). For the other 339 laws the instance decides the law for N <= 5, among them associativity, and (N <= 10) left-distributivity. The first version reported 18 insufficient and 31 undecided laws because of a classification bug - a law whose enumeration was capped at one N was counted as undecided even when a violation had been found at another N; the Skeptic's independent census gives the same 339 / 43 / 0 as the corrected experiment.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C17-08: For every N, the full anchor instance of left-distributivity has a model iff N is a power of 2 (rediscovery; Lean, from Laver's theorem)

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 proof for every N with Laver's theorem as an explicit hypothesis - proofs/lean/Infinity/C17_RowPeriod.lean, full_instance_iff_pow_two and only_powers_of_two (standard axioms only): if for every j some anchored table of order 2^j satisfies the full instance (Laver's published theorem that A_n is left-distributive, the input of CLM-C06-01 and CLM-C17-05, used only at order 2^(v2(N)+1)), then an anchored table of order N satisfies it iff N is a power of 2; written proof proofs/paper/C17.md Theorem 5 (the Historian's row-period argument, completed by the Skeptic, reviews/skeptic_batch1.1.md section 1.2, which also wrote the Lean file); numerically checked for every N <= 4096 (EXP-C17-03 and both reviewers' scripts)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For every N >= 1, an anchored table (x*0 = x + 1 mod N) satisfies x*(y+1) = (x*y)*(x+1) for all x and y, the wrap-around y = N - 1 included, iff N is a power of 2; it is then the Laver table A_n. Equivalently (CLM-C17-05), left-distributivity of an anchored table of any order is decided by an O(N^2) comparison with the forced table.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C17.md#theorem-5-clm-c17-08--the-full-anchor-instance-has-a-model-iff-n-is-a-power-of-2
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C17_RowPeriod.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Stated in Biane, Sem. Lothar. Combin. 80 (2019) B80c, section 2.1, footnote 1 (credited to A. Lewandowski) and, as Biane's observation, in Aguilera-Iannella, 'Non-dyadic Laver algebras', arXiv:2610.06106 (2026), section 2.2. A proof: their Prop. 2.5 (read; the instance at (a, N) holds iff a * N = N) with Dehornoy, Braids and Self-Distributivity (2000), Lemma X.1.7 (left-distributive iff a * N = N for every a) and Prop. X.1.6 (no such left-distributive table unless N is a power of 2), the last two read only as cited by Aguilera-Iannella (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md, section 'The CLM-C17-05 question').

- **references:** https://www.mat.univie.ac.at/~slc/wpapers/s80biane.pdf; https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06106
- **limitations:**

  Conditional on Laver's theorem (published); the lab checks the Laver tables only computationally (A_n left-distributive for n <= 12 in EXP-C06-01). The literature chain through Dehornoy's book (read only as cited) is no longer needed.

- **related:** CLM-C17-05; CLM-C06-01
- **notes:** Answers the first of the C17 next directions ('prove the LD anchor-instance characterisation for all N') from the literature.
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-20T02:03:31Z verification: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED → PROVEN (sync:C17; Skeptic review of batch 1.1 (reviews/skeptic_batch1.1.md, section 1) - the Historian's row-period proof completed and machine-checked (C17_RowPeriod.lean, conditional on Laver's theorem): COMPUTATION…)

### CLM-C18-01: e-transformations are bijections of strings of fixed length

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (eT_injective, eT_length; standard axioms only) plus the finite-cardinality step (written proof, proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 1); d(e(X)) = X for all seven quasigroups (EXP-C18-01)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For every quasigroup Q, leader l and length n, the e-transformation e_l is a bijection of Q^n, inverted by the d-transformation a_i = b_(i-1) \ b_i; hence every composition of forward and reversed e-transformations is a bijection of Q^n.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-1-clm-c18-01--e-transformations-are-bijections
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C18_Quasigroups.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  S. Markovski, D. Gligoroski, V. Bakeva, Quasigroup string processing, Part 1, Contributions, Sec. Math. Tech. Sci., MANU (1999) - the E- and D-transformations with the same leader are mutually inverse permutations of strings (as cited in US patent 8041031, https://image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/8041031, and in Markovski-Bakeva, Quasigroup String Processing Part 4, https://www.academia.edu/115482463/Quasigroup_String_Processing_Part_4).


### CLM-C18-02: Differences propagate forward only; forward-only tail hashes fail the strict avalanche criterion

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib for the persistence of differences (eT_ne_of_ne; standard axioms only); agreement before i and the corollary by written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 2); 0 violations on 7 x 2000 random strings (EXP-C18-01); max bias 0.5 for all three forward-only variants (EXP-C18-02)

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  If u and v differ exactly at position i, then e_l(u) and e_l(v) agree at positions < i and differ at every position >= i. Consequently, in a hash that outputs the last 8 bytes of forward e-transformations only, changing a message byte at position i >= n - 8 leaves the earlier tail bytes unchanged, so some output bits never flip and the maximal avalanche bias is 1/2.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-2-clm-c18-02--a-difference-propagates-forward-and-only-forward
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C18_Quasigroups.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-01; EXP-C18-02
- **novelty_basis:** Elementary; implicit in the quasigroup-string-processing literature.

### CLM-C18-03: The tail-information bound: an e-transformation tail carries one byte of state

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib for the factorisation of the tail through one state (eT_append, eT_tail_factors) and injectivity (eT_injective); the counting consequences by written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 3); the bound is attained exactly (256 values) in 100 families of 4096 keys (EXP-C18-05) and explains the 32,624 full collisions of EXP-C18-03 exactly

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  The last k outputs of an e-transformation are a bijective function of the state before them and the last k
  inputs. Hence a hash outputting the last 8 bytes after p forward e-transformations takes at most 256^p values on
  messages sharing their last 7 bytes, and with a final reversed e-transformation it takes at most 256 values on
  messages sharing their first 7 bytes, for every p and every quasigroup of order 256.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-3-clm-c18-03--the-tail-information-bound
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C18_Quasigroups.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-03; EXP-C18-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  An elementary narrow-pipe property of byte-level e-transformations. Quasigroup hash designs submitted to the SHA-3 competition used huge quasigroups in wide-pipe constructions (Edon-R, orders 2^256-2^512, https://ehash.isec.tugraz.at/uploads/7/74/Edon.pdf; NaSHA, https://eprint.iacr.org/2008/519), which avoids it; two searches found no explicit statement of the bound for byte-level e-transformation hashes.


### CLM-C18-04: T-function quasigroups (affine mod 256, XOR, (x + 1) XOR y) cannot mix high input bits into low output bits

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 4, induction along the chains using x = x' and y = y' mod 2^r => x*y = x'*y' mod 2^r); 0 low-bit changes for AFF, XOR, ALP in EXP-C18-01; max bias 0.5 in EXP-C18-02

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If x*y is a T-function on Z/2^8, then in every QG hash (any number of forward and reversed e-transformations) bit j of every output byte depends only on bits 0..j of the message bytes; flipping an input bit at position r never changes an output bit at a position < r, and the maximal avalanche bias is 1/2.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-4-clm-c18-04--the-t-function-obstruction
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-01; EXP-C18-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Closure of T-functions under composition - A. Klimov, A. Shamir, A new class of invertible mappings, CHES 2002; see https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-function and https://www.iacr.org/archive/fse2004/30170001/30170001.pdf.


### CLM-C18-05: GF(2)-affine quasigroups give GF(2)-affine hashes with key-independent differences

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 5); one distinct output difference over 2000 keys (EXP-C18-01); mean bias 0.5 for QG-XOR and for CRC32 (EXP-C18-02)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:** If x*y = A(x) XOR B(y) XOR c with A, B linear over GF(2) (for example x XOR y), every QG hash satisfies H(M XOR D) = H(M) XOR L(D) for a fixed linear L; every avalanche cell is 0 or 1.
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-5-clm-c18-05--gf2-affine-quasigroups-give-affine-hashes
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-01; EXP-C18-02
- **novelty_basis:** Folklore (affine maps compose to affine maps; the same property makes CRCs unsuitable as hash-table mixers).

### CLM-C18-06: The anchored quasigroup x + c(y): additive differences persist, and the one-pass hash is anagram-blind

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 6); 2000/2000 one-pass hashes unchanged under prefix permutations (EXP-C18-01); bit independence 1.0 with ideal per-bit flip rates for QG-ARP p2+rev (EXP-C18-02); 36,720 full collisions for QG-ARP p1 on near-duplicates (EXP-C18-03)

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  For x*y = x + c(y) mod 256, two runs over the same inputs from states b, b' differ by the constant b' - b at every position, and one pass computes prefix sums l + sum c(a_i). Hence the one-pass tail hash is unchanged by permutations of the message bytes before the tail, and with a reverse pass all 8 output bytes change by one common additive constant when a message byte at position >= 8 changes (perfectly correlated bit changes).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-6-clm-c18-06--the-anchored-quasigroup-x--cy-keeps-additive-differences
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-01; EXP-C18-02; EXP-C18-03
- **novelty_basis:** Elementary; shows that an anchored quasigroup of C06 (Theorem 4 there) is a poor mixing primitive.

### CLM-C18-07: The 8-pass cascade has a bijective 64-bit state update

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 7, triangular composition of row and column bijections); quality of the cascades measured separately (EXP-C18-05)
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  In the cascade QC(T, d) (d e-transformations run in parallel, output = the final state of every pass), the update of the state (s_1, ..., s_d) by one input byte is a bijection of Q^d; so states are never merged and the tail-information bound does not apply to the output.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-7-clm-c18-07--the-cascade-keeps-a-full-8-byte-state
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-05

### CLM-C18-08: H-C18-1: QG-LS with 2 passes and a reverse pass has BLAKE2b-level avalanche

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** QG-LS p2+rev has mean and maximal avalanche bias within 0.01 of BLAKE2b-64 on 2000 random 16-byte keys.
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-02
- **limitations:**

  Holds (0.0089 / 0.041 versus 0.0089 / 0.045, also with one forward pass), but the same hash fails every structured distribution test (CLM-C18-03) - single-bit avalanche statistics cannot see the tail-information bound.


### CLM-C18-09: H-C18-2: structured quasigroups are worse mixers than a random Latin square (maximal avalanche bias)

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** INCONCLUSIVE
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  Every structured quasigroup tested (affine, XOR, x + c(y), (x + 1) XOR y) gives a larger maximal avalanche bias than the random Latin square, in the reverse-pass (p2+rev) construction. (As first worded - 'in the same construction' - the hypothesis is false for the forward-only construction, where the random Latin square and x + c(y) both have bias exactly 1/2 by CLM-C18-02; the experiment compared every structured variant with QG-LS p2+rev.)

- **evidence:** EXP-C18-02
- **limitations:**

  Proved (bias 1/2) for the T-function and GF(2)-affine quasigroups (CLM-C18-04, CLM-C18-05). For x + c(y) with a reverse pass the difference (0.043 versus 0.041) is within sampling noise; its defect shows in bit independence instead (CLM-C18-06), which the hypothesis did not measure.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C18-10: H-C18-3: the nimber-field quasigroup gives a hash competitive with FNV-1a (refuted)

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** QG-NIMINV p2+rev (x*y = (x XOR y)^-1 in the nimber field of order 256) has avalanche at least as good as FNV-1a-64, |chi-square z| <= 3 and a bucket-collision ratio within 2% on all four key sets.
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-02; EXP-C18-03
- **counterexample:**

  Avalanche is better than FNV-1a's (max bias 0.0415 vs 0.5), but on the decimal strings 0..65535 the 1024-bucket z-score is 1084 and the bucket-collision ratio 2.62, and near-duplicate keys give 32,624 full 64-bit collisions - the tail-information bound (CLM-C18-03). (FNV-1a itself has z = -9.8 there - more uniform than random, bucket-collision ratio 0.995 - and 0 full collisions; the first wording wrongly attributed |z| <= 3 on all four key sets to FNV-1a.)

- **status history:**

### CLM-C18-11: H-C18-8: the 8-pass cascade over LS, ISO and NIMINV passes every test (refuted)

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  The cascades QC-LS, QC-ISO and QC-NIMINV (depth 8, 8 zero bytes of finalisation) have avalanche within 0.01 of BLAKE2b-64, |z| <= 3, collision ratios within 2% and no full collisions on all four key sets.

- **evidence:** EXP-C18-05
- **counterexample:**

  QC-NIMINV d8 - maximal avalanche bias 0.0845 against 0.045 for BLAKE2b (about 7 standard deviations of a cell; the Skeptic's replication with new keys - 0.0735, against a null maximum of 0.048; worst cell key byte 15, bit 0 -> top bit of s1). QC-LS d8 and QC-ISO d8 pass every test (EXP-C18-05). (A z = -3.21 on synthetic words was also cited at first; it is 1 of 48 z-scores and in the benign direction - more uniform than random - so the refutation rests on the avalanche bias alone.)

- **status history:**

- **cycle:** C18

### CLM-C18-13: Quasigroup hashes against non-cryptographic baselines - no significant improvement

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** algorithmic
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **improvement:** NO IMPROVEMENT
- **statement:**

  Among 13 tail-output quasigroup hashes and 4 cascades, measured against FNV-1a-64, MX64 (multiply-xorshift with the splitmix64 finaliser), BLAKE2b-64 and CRC32 on avalanche, bit independence, chi-square, collisions and throughput, no quasigroup construction improves significantly on the best baseline in any measured dimension (some have numerically smaller maximal bias or bit-independence values, within noise).

- **evidence:** EXP-C18-02; EXP-C18-03; EXP-C18-04; EXP-C18-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  Quasigroup string processing for cryptographic primitives and pseudo-random generators is established (Markovski et al.; Gligoroski, Candidate one-way functions and one-way permutations based on quasigroup string transformations, https://ia.cr/2005/352; Edon-R); the benchmark against these baselines is ours.

- **limitations:** Python/numpy implementations on a shared machine (times indicative); a small subset of SMHasher-style tests; one random Latin square (not uniformly sampled); 16-byte keys for avalanche.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C19-01: Every Zeckendorf game has length B(n) - sum_{i>=2} (i-1) MC_i

- **cycle:** C19
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C19.md Theorem 1; potential Psi(c) = sum c_i a_i); the per-move changes of Psi and their sum over an arbitrary move list are Lean-verified (dPsi_C1, dPsi_S2, dPsi_split, dPsi_combine, length_identity; standard axioms only); checked on all greedy games n < 1500 and 1500 random games (EXP-C19-02)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For every game G on n (from n copies of F_1 to the Zeckendorf representation), |G| = B(n) - sum_{i>=2} (i-1) MC_i(G),
  where B(n) = sum_i (F_{i+2} - i - 2) delta_i is the bound of Cusenza et al. and MC_i counts the combines
  F_{i-1} + F_i -> F_{i+1}. Hence a game is longest iff it minimises the weighted number of such combines, and
  L(n) = B(n) - D(n) with D(n) = min_G sum (i-1) MC_i(G).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C19.md#theorem-1-clm-c19-01--an-exact-length-identity
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C19_Zeckendorf.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C19-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Stated as the exact game length in A. Cusenza, A. Dunkelberg, K. Huffman, D. Ke, M. McClatchey, S. J. Miller, C. Mizgerd, V. Tiwari, J. Ye, X. Zheng, Bounds on Zeckendorf games, Fibonacci Quart. 60 (2022), no. 1, 57-71, eq. (3.16) (derived by linear algebra and then weakened to obtain their Thm 1.3; arXiv:2009.09510); also used as an inequality step in J. Cheigh et al., Towards the Gaussianity of random Zeckendorf games, CANT VI, Springer 2025. The one-line potential proof for every game is ours.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C19-02: The split phase of the longest game ends at the lazy Fibonacci representation

- **cycle:** C19
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C19.md Theorem 2 - abelian property, an explicit carry lemma by induction, increment of the lazy representation); verified for every n < 5000 and for 300 random firing orders (EXP-C19-02); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C13_C19.md) - from every position of value <= 200 all maximal split sequences have one end point and one length

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  From n copies of F_1, every maximal sequence of split moves (F_1 + F_1 -> F_2, F_2 + F_2 -> F_1 + F_3,
  F_i + F_i -> F_{i-2} + F_{i+1}) has the same length and ends at the lazy representation of n - the unique
  representation as a sum of distinct F_i (F_1 = 1, F_2 = 2, ...) without two consecutive missing indices below
  the largest one.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C19.md#theorem-2-clm-c19-02--the-split-phase-ends-at-the-lazy-representation
- **evidence:** EXP-C19-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Searches (2026-10-18) for the Zeckendorf game together with lazy representations / chip-firing found no such statement; the papers on the game (Baird-Smith et al. arXiv:1809.04881; Li et al. arXiv:2006.16457; Cusenza et al. arXiv:2009.09510; Cheigh et al. 2025; Black Hole Zeckendorf games arXiv:2409.10981) do not mention lazy representations. Small; uses the classical uniqueness of lazy representations (J. L. Brown, 1965). All arXiv Zeckendorf-game papers to 2026-10-07 were checked (including the reversed, ordered and black-hole variants); none mentions lazy representations (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C19-03: Closed form of the longest game on a Fibonacci number: L(F_k) = F_{k+2} - k - 2 - floor((k-1)^2/4)

- **cycle:** C19
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C19.md Theorem 4) from CLM-C19-01, CLM-C19-02 and the published optimality of the split-first strategy (Cusenza et al. 2022, Thm 1.2); the weight sums are Lean-verified (weight_even, weight_odd, quarter_square_even, quarter_square_odd); checked for every level j <= 33 (EXP-C19-03) and by exhaustive search for n <= 150 (EXP-C19-01); skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C13_C19.md) - the Skeptic read [C, Thm 1.2] in the arXiv and published versions (it says exactly what the proof uses) and confirmed the formula by strategy-free exhaustive search up to F_12 = 233 (L = 566, 13.8 million positions)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  With F_1 = 1, F_2 = 2, F_{i+1} = F_i + F_{i-1}, the longest Zeckendorf game on n = F_k has length
  F_{k+2} - k - 2 - floor((k-1)^2/4) (0, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 37, 67, 117, 201, ...), i.e. its deficit to the bound
  B(F_k) is the quarter-square floor((k-1)^2/4).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C19.md#theorem-4-clm-c19-03--the-longest-game-on-a-fibonacci-number
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C19_Zeckendorf.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C19-01; EXP-C19-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Cusenza et al. (2022) give the exact length only for n = F_k - 1 (their Thm 1.4) and a bound otherwise; Cheigh et al. (2025) state that a closed form for non-Fibonacci inputs is open and describe the Fibonacci case as treated by Cusenza et al., whose paper we read in full and which treats F_k - 1, not F_k. The sequence L(F_k) is not in the OEIS (EXP-C19-04). Medium-low confidence that no formula exists in the literature. Confirmed 2026-10-19: Cusenza et al. (arXiv:2009.09510) contain no formula for n = F_k; no later Zeckendorf-game paper treats it (arXiv to 2026-10-07); L(F_k) is still not in the OEIS (Historian audit 2026-10-19, reviews/historian_C11_C20.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C19-04: A short proof that only n = F_k - 1 can be played with split moves alone (Cusenza et al., Thm 1.4)

- **cycle:** C19
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C19.md Corollary 3, from CLM-C19-02 - a split-only game ends at the lazy representation, which equals the Zeckendorf representation exactly for alternating digit strings); consistent with EXP-C19-01 (bound attained exactly at F_k - 1 for n <= 150)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:** The Zeckendorf game on n can be played with split and combine-1 moves only iff n = F_k - 1 for some k >= 2.
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C19.md#corollary-3-clm-c19-04--a-short-proof-of-c-thm-14
- **evidence:** EXP-C19-01; EXP-C19-02
- **novelty_basis:** The statement is Theorem 1.4 of Cusenza et al., Fib. Quart. 60 (2022), arXiv:2009.09510; the proof via lazy representations is new to us and much shorter.

### CLM-C19-05: A two-sequence recursion for the longest Zeckendorf game on every n

- **cycle:** C19
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  Let h(k) = floor(k^2/4) and define rho_p (p = 0, 1) by rho_p(0) = 0 and, for m = F_{i+1} + t with Zeckendorf top
  level i (0 <= t < F_i): rho_p(m) = r_p(i) + [i = p mod 2] * max(0, rho_p(t) - h(i-2)), where r_p(0) = 1,
  r_p(i) = h(i) + 2 for i >= 1, i = p mod 2, and r_p(i) = h(i+1) + 1 otherwise. Then D(0) = 0 and
  D(F_{j+1} + m) = h(j) + D(m) - rho_{j mod 2}(m) for 0 <= m < F_j, and L(n) = B(n) - D(n).

- **evidence:** EXP-C19-03; EXP-C19-04; EXP-C19-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  Cheigh et al. (CANT VI, Springer 2025) state that a convenient closed form for the longest game on non-Fibonacci inputs is open; no such formula found in the papers above or the OEIS (L(n) has no OEIS entry, EXP-C19-04).

- **limitations:**

  Inferred from all n < 30000 (in-sample) and confirmed out of sample on 6,272 further n up to 2*10^7 (EXP-C19-04) and on 103,799 n - all n < 10^5, 3000 random n < 10^15 and 800 numbers F_j + m - against the provably correct lazy-representation method (EXP-C19-05, Theorem 7); the Skeptic also checked it against strategy-free exhaustive search for n <= 200 and n = 233. No proof. The data beyond exhaustive search rely on the published optimality of the strategy (Cusenza et al., Thm 1.2).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C19-06: The deficit of a two-summand number depends only on the lower level and the parity of the gap

- **cycle:** C19
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For levels j >= i + 2, D(F_{j+1} + F_{i+1}) = floor(j^2/4) + floor(i^2/4) - R(j,i) with R(j,0) = 1,
  R(j,i) = floor((i+1)^2/4) + 1 if j - i is odd and R(j,i) = floor(i^2/4) + 2 if j - i is even (i >= 1).

- **evidence:** EXP-C19-03
- **novelty_basis:** Special case of CLM-C19-05; not found in the literature searched.
- **limitations:** Checked for all 276 pairs with j <= 24 (the Skeptic - all 1,770 pairs with j <= 60); no proof.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C19-08: Every multiset of Fibonacci numbers with sum n is a reachable game position

- **cycle:** C19
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C19.md Proposition 6 - undoing combines reaches n copies of F_1; read backwards this is a forward game); state counts equal the partition counts for every n <= 150 (EXP-C19-01)

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** The positions reachable from n copies of F_1 are exactly the multisets of Fibonacci numbers F_1, F_2, ... with sum n; their number is the number of partitions of n into Fibonacci parts (OEIS A003107).
